Plausibility: medium-high for our base, medium-low for the subsidiary's €422m
North America Interactive stays profitable and builds to a 7.5% margin by FY2030 (our base, consistent with consensus revenue) Breakeven reached in Q2 2026 (+$3.0m on revenue +16.9%); variable-cost platforms; Rhode Island's online monopoly (+23.3% in July 2026) Scale against FanDuel and DraftKings; absorbing acquisition costs inflated about 40% by prediction-market keyword bidding Rush Street shows a gaming-led, disciplined operator can sustain above-average margins; experts call Bally's "significantly behind" with low single-digit share. Plausibility: medium, and immaterial to the group (about $9m to $29m of EBITDAR)
Interest stays near $460m a year (consensus, implied) None; no financing since 2024 has been on better terms than the last (sale-leasebacks at 8% to 8.5%, 11% notes in 2025, SOFR plus 7.50% in February 2026, SOFR plus 8.50% in October 2026) Access to cheaper capital The unsecured notes trade at 63% to 70% of par; private-credit lenders (Ares, King Street, TPG, WhiteHawk) set the marginal price. Plausibility: low; our $582m is more plausible than consensus
The parent closes the gap between earnings and fixed charges without a capital-structure event (the implicit assumption behind FY2028 consensus free cash flow of +$44m) Lease coverage of 1.98x and 2.13x at the property level; GLPI's willingness to fund; the Intralot monetisation (€1.53bn of cash in 2025) A deleveraging path from lease-adjusted leverage of 11x to 14x Precedents searched: Station Casinos (Chapter 11, 2009 to 2011), Caesars' operating company (2015 to 2017), Tropicana Entertainment (2008), Trump Entertainment (2009), Revel (2012 and 2014), Century Casinos (asset sales and a $7.5m rent cut in 2026), Golden Entertainment (a sale-leaseback at low leverage). None closed a gap of this kind from this starting point without an equity injection, a large sale or a restructuring. Plausibility: very low

2. Management capacity, the strength beneath all the others. Four finance heads in under four years (Lavan, Glover, Mircheva, Papanier on an interim basis), a treasurer gone since April 2026 and unreplaced, a CEO running two listed companies, and a controlling shareholder who is now Executive Chair. The capacity to run a refinancing, a Chicago opening, an Evoke integration (at the subsidiary) and the Bronx at once is not demonstrated. Peers executing comparable programmes (PENN's relocations, Caesars' post-merger deleveraging) did so with stable finance teams. The AlphaSense report rates its own confidence on this section "low", which is itself the point: there is no evidence of the capacity, only of its absence.

3. What is demonstrably strong, and why it does not carry the case. The two genuinely demonstrated strengths, UK online and the property-level profitability of the regional estate (rent coverage above Caesars' 1.61x and PENN's Pinnacle lease at 1.70x, below Boyd's 2.46x), accrue respectively to a 58%-owned ring-fenced subsidiary and to the landlord's security. The strengths are real; they are not the parent equity's. This is the first set's point (the advantages are in the wrong places), confirmed by the parent cash-flow report.

4. For the forecasts. Our base case is plausible on demonstrated strengths; the lean toward delay and shortfall in the second set is justified by this evidence, and I would not lean it further. The two elements of our own base that still rest on undemonstrated strengths are Chicago reaching $150m of stabilised EBITDAR by FY2030 (the temporary casino's $13m is no guide either way) and the debt bridge being available at about 13% at all. Consensus's expected change (EBITDA +11.7% in FY2027 and +16.6% in FY2028; positive free cash flow in FY2028) rests on strengths the company has not shown, which is why I expect it to be cut (I-02).

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

F1515th of 15

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: The cone has two layers, and they must be kept apart. The operating layer (revenue and EBITDA after rent, before any capital-structure event) is moderately wide and only mildly skewed: FY2027 EBITDA after rent runs from about $380m (bear, about 25% weight) through $459m (base, about 50%) to $560m to $610m (bull, about 25%), with the probability skewed down (I put about 55% on an outcome below base) and the magnitude skewed up (the one large upside, a Deutsche Bank-like Chicago year one, is worth more than the several small downsides). The equity layer is very wide and skewed hard down. Adjusted EPS on the debt-bridge branch alone runs from about -$10.1 to about -$6.3 around -$8.8 (model -$9.08 before minorities), and the capital-structure branches then overlay outcomes from a modest dilution to a wipe-out. My branch weights are a debt bridge 40%, a sponsor-led recapitalisation with third-party capital 20%, a restructuring 30%, a take-private 5% and a clean refinancing 5%; against the second set I move 10 points from the bridge and the take-private to the restructuring, because Standard General's own capital ($647m of discretionary assets) is too small to fund a rescue alone, the revolver has already shrunk to about $319m, the unsecured notes price a haircut at 63% to 70% of par, and the first-lien covenant cannot be met by operations in May 2027 on any plausible EBITDA. The case is therefore moderately predictable as an operating forecast and unpredictable as an equity forecast: the probability that the common equity is worth materially less in 18 months than today is higher than the probability that it is worth more, but the size of the upside tail (a sponsor bid or a benign exchange that re-rates the equity sharply from a thin float) is large enough that the distribution is not a simple decline.

1. The operating cone for FY2027 (base case inputs, before the capital-structure branch).

Bear (about 25%) Base (about 50%) Bull (about 25%)
Chicago Opens Q4 2027 or later; weak ramp; liquidated damages accrue beyond the extension window (up to about $12m a month on the city's projected tax, my calculation on its $200m-a-year stabilised figure, low confidence) Opens start of Q3 2027; year one annualised EBITDAR $65m against $80m of new rent Opens April 2027 at Deutsche Bank-like economics (about $160m to $250m annualised first-year EBITDAR)
UK B2C Netherlands-style leakage: revenue -4%, margin 27% Revenue +2.8%, margin 29.1% Growth +6% on share gains from exiting rivals; margin 30%
Core casinos -2%; margin -50bp; Missouri and Atlantic City share loss accelerates Flat; margin -30bp +1.5% on a resilient drive-in consumer (Boyd saw "no material impact" from fuel prices)

Two features of the table matter more than its edges. First, a Chicago delay is EBITDA-positive and cash-negative in FY2027 (no rent, the temporary casino keeps trading, but construction carry, possible section 7.4 damages and a longer wait for the permanent casino's cash), so the "bear" for cash is not the "bear" for EBITDA; the memo should not read a Chicago slip as a profit miss. Second, the bull case rests on a single driver, so it is a lump of probability at one end, not a tail that thins smoothly; the AlphaSense dispersion report's reading ("the one large upside driver is binary in its economics") is the right one.

2. The equity cone: the branches and what each does to the claim.

Branch and weight What triggers it Effect on the common equity Precedent
A. C. E. Clean refinancing below a 10% blended cost, 5% Credit markets reopen; a Chicago opening on time reprices the credit The first set's "change my mind" trigger; interest falls, the equity re-rates None recent at this leverage

Branches B, C and D have one thing in common: the terms are set by Standard General, the lenders and GLPI, and the minority's protections are thin (a controlled company, an Executive Chair who is the sponsor's chief investment officer, no lead independent director, and a staggered board; the majority-of-the-minority vote that protected the 2025 merger is not required for a recapitalisation).

3. The skew, stated plainly. For EBITDA after rent in FY2027, the modal outcome is slightly below the base and the mean slightly above it because of the Chicago lump. For free cash flow, every branch except E is negative in every year and the skew is down. For the equity, the distribution is bimodal: a 55% to 60% weight on outcomes that leave the common worth between roughly nothing and today's value (B and D, part of A), a 35% to 40% weight on survival at or near today's value with a larger claim pending (most of A), and a 5% to 10% weight on a sharp re-rating (C, E, or a benign exchange). The first set's sum-of-the-parts (central value negative; $5 to $8 a share in a survival case with dilution; $0 to $2 in a restructuring) is consistent with this and I do not change it.

4. What narrows the cone, in date order.

  1. Bally's Q3 2026 results and 10-Q (within days): liquidity against the $325m minimum, the revolver balance after the October step-down, any change to the going-concern language, Chicago's stated opening date, whether a third late-filing notice appears.
  2. Bally's Intralot's Q3 results (27 November 2026): UK constant-currency growth after the generosity cuts (above 6% confirms the base; below 3% puts about $30m to $40m of FY2027 B2C revenue at risk), and whether the €422m guidance survives.
  3. The Chicago host-agreement deadline (10 December 2026) and the extensions, the temporary-licence extension, and the first city video-terminal permits.
  4. The FY2026 results and 10-K (late February or March 2027): the auditor's going-concern opinion, the Q1 2027 covenant outlook, the refinancing or amendment terms (amount, coupon, security, any equity, any Standard General participation), and the Evoke completion mechanics (the cash-or-share split against the £117.1m cap).
  5. The Q1 2027 compliance certificate (May 2027): the first-lien test at 4.50x, revolver utilisation against the 25% threshold, any equity cure.
  6. The Chicago opening and the first two months of Illinois Gaming Board receipts (mid-2027), read against Rivers Des Plaines (about $47m a month) and Wind Creek (about $20m).
  7. The April 2027 betting-duty step and the Ohio exit (30 June 2027); the Bronx construction-start deadline (September 2027) and the Evoke 2028 refinancing.

5. For the forecasts. Give the FY2027 operating numbers medium-low weight (±15% on EBITDA after rent) and FY2027 EPS low weight. Do not present a single EPS path for FY2028 onward without the branch it belongs to. Where the memo needs one number for the equity, use the enterprise value and the claim of each security on it, not EPS; the model's two-year-forward price-to-earnings return calculation (a guideline multiple of 0.0x on negative earnings, giving a meaningless -1,607% return) cannot be used and the memo should say so (D-06).

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

F3814th of 15

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: The company's own disclosure is poor on exactly the items that decide the case, but the case can still be monitored well, because the decisive drivers are disclosed by third parties on their own calendars, several of them monthly. Bally's gives no guidance, no parent-only cash flow, no segment detail for Chicago in its own filings, no currency sensitivity, has filed both 2026 quarterlies late, has ineffective disclosure controls and a material weakness in tax accounting, and its informal commentary ("development liquidity ensured", "on track") sits well above its filings. Against that, GLPI's quarterly reports give Chicago funding, rent coverage and public warnings; Bally's Chicago, Inc. files its own 10-Q with the temporary casino's EBITDAR; the Illinois Gaming Board publishes monthly receipts by casino; Rhode Island and the other states publish monthly gaming revenue; Bally's Intralot reports quarterly in Athens with UK growth, mitigation and guidance; the credit agreement's amendment is on file with the liquidity minimums; and the unsecured bonds trade. The gap that no third party fills is the parent's own liquidity and the refinancing negotiation, which are the two things that matter most over six months and which the Consumer will learn about only when Bally's chooses to say, or when a filing forces it. Net, monitoring is feasible and mostly timely for the operating drivers, and blind for the financing driver until the event.

2. Three disclosure traps to watch for over the six months.

  • Basis traps. Bally's reports Adjusted EBITDA after rent; FactSet's history is operating EBITDA ($184m against $247m for H1 2026); Bally's Intralot reports IFRS adjusted EBITDA in euros with leases below EBITDA. The model's quarterly phasing already shows what a basis mismatch does (Note 4). Read every print against its own prior-year figure on the same basis.
  • Reported against adjusted. The gap has run $1 to $3 a share a year (Q1 2026 carried a $105.8m sale-leaseback gain, a $21.1m FX gain and a $169.3m non-operating charge). Q3 2026 may carry Sam's Town transaction costs and Star fair-value moves. The headline will mislead in one direction or the other.
  • Perimeter changes. Evoke's consolidation (about $1.75bn of annualised revenue and about $420m of EBITDA at the subsidiary) will inflate reported growth and sell-side revision statistics in 2027 without changing the parent's claim (I-02). Sam's Town adds a property with no disclosed price.

3. What the company could disclose that would change the score, and why it will not. A parent-only sources-and-uses, Chicago's opening budget and ramp assumptions, the liquidity position monthly, and a statement of Standard General's intentions would lift the score to the 60s. None is likely: the company is negotiating with lenders, the city and a landlord at once, and disclosure is being managed for those audiences, not for minority shareholders. The first set's conclusion (C-60, C-73) that GLPI and Bally's Intralot are the better channels stands and is now stronger, because Bally's Chicago, Inc.'s separate filings are a third such channel.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

F3015th of 15

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: What the company says it will achieve does not trace into numbers, because the company says almost nothing in numbers; what the value chain does to the company traces into numbers very clearly, down to the pre-tax line, and then loses clarity at the per-share line because the share count and the claim on the enterprise depend on a capital-structure event the company has not described. The internal chain (milestone to driver to line to EPS) is therefore clear only where we have built it ourselves from third-party disclosure: Chicago's opening quarter sets the rent step ($80m a year from the first month), the receipts set the EBITDAR, the margin assumption sets the net; the UK mitigation rate sets the B2C margin; the refinancing terms set the interest line. The external chain (landlord yields, lender spreads, fiscal policy, municipal politics) is the clearest part of the whole case: each link is contractual or statutory and can be sized in advance. The break in both chains is the same: from pre-tax profit to earnings per share, where minorities, the four EPS bases, the penny warrants, the non-cash accretion and the branch-dependent share count mean that two analysts with the same operating view can report FY2027 EPS anywhere from about -$2.9 to -$9.1, which is exactly the spread of the five brokers on FactSet. For the memo, EPS is the wrong unit of transmission for this company; EBITDA after rent, fixed-charge coverage and enterprise value are the right ones.

1. What the company says it will achieve, and how far each statement traces.

Statement (source and date) Operational driver it implies Line it should move Traceable to EPS?
Chicago permanent casino "on track" to open in spring or H1 2027 (September 2026 update call; Deutsche Bank, 2 November 2026) A gaming floor of about 4,000 positions opening with about 100 rooms; the temporary casino closing Casinos & Resorts revenue (+$238m in FY2027, +$375m in FY2028 in our base); rent +$80m a year from opening; EBITDAR at a 20% year-one margin Only through our own build; the company gives no revenue, EBITDAR, rent-start, promotional or database assumptions. The pattern: the company's statements are milestones and adjectives. The numbers that would make them forecasts (Chicago's opening budget, ramp, rent timing, promotional reinvestment; the parent's sources and uses; the refinancing size and price) are absent. The first set's score of 15 for communication (C-60) is confirmed.

2. The internal transmission as we have built it (base case, FY2027 against FY2026). Revenue +$178m (Chicago +$88m, Sam's Town +$42m, B2C +$27m, North America Interactive +$31m, B2B -$11m, core flat) leads to EBITDAR +$30m (Chicago +$26m, mitigation and synergies +$15m to +$20m, North America Interactive +$9m, B2B -$7m, core -$6m) leads to rent +$54m (Chicago -$40m for the half year, escalators -$14m) leads to EBITDA after rent -$24m leads to interest +$105m (reset repricing $26m, WhiteHawk and new money $74m, accretion flat) leads to pre-tax -$147m worse leads to adjusted EPS -$2.5 worse. Every step is traceable and each has a named observable (the opening month; the Illinois Gaming Board's receipts; the 8-K on the amendment). What cannot be traced is which share count to divide by.

3. The external transmission, link by link.

  • Landlord to EPS: GLPI's 8.5% yield on $940m plus the land rent and 1% to 2% escalators are contractual; each $100m of GLPI funding is $8.5m of rent, or about $0.14 a share. The clearest link in the case.
  • Lenders to EPS: the term-loan margin (SOFR plus 7.50%), the WhiteHawk margin (SOFR plus 8.50%) and the swaps fix most of the current interest; the unknown is the reset and the new money. Each 100 basis points on about $3bn of parent debt is about $0.49 a share. Clear in mechanism, unknown in size until signed.
  • Fiscal policy to EPS: the UK duty steps are statutory; each point of Remote Gaming Duty costs about 1% of Bally's Intralot's EBITDA (Deutsche Bank, October 2024); Bally's takes 58%. Chicago's 23.2% local casino tax against the 5.15% video-terminal rate, the 10.25% municipal online betting tax and the Illinois per-wager tax are all legislated. Clear.
  • Municipal politics to EPS: the video-terminal rollout (78 state licences, no city permits yet) and the property-tax incentive ($27m a year, opposed by the mayor; 13% of Deutsche Bank's 2027 Chicago EBITDAR) are observable but not sizeable in advance; Bally's own figure (30% to 50% cannibalisation) is advocacy, and Illinois' history (casino revenue -3% a year compound after 2012 as terminals grew to $1.7bn) is the better guide. Medium clarity.
  • Consumer to EPS: regional gross gaming revenue to Casinos & Resorts revenue at about 55% flow-through; each 1% of core revenue is about $0.13 a share. Clear and small.
  • Currency to EPS: about 28% to 32% of revenue in sterling; a 10% move is about $29m of B2C EBITDAR before the 58% look-through; the sterling swap expired in October 2026 and the company publishes no sensitivity. Medium clarity.

4. Why EPS breaks the chain, and what to use instead. Four EPS bases exist (reported basic, FactSet adjusted, brokers' adjusted, ours); the brokers' FY2027 figures span -$2.87 (Truist, which on $541m of EBITDA must be excluding acquired-intangible amortisation or minorities or both) to -$6.79; our adjusted figure is -$8.81 after minorities and -$9.08 before; the penny warrants (11.62m) are in some counts and not others (FactSet's 48.5m excludes them); reported interest carries about $85m a year of non-cash accretion; and a recapitalisation could add 62m shares. None of this is a forecasting disagreement; it is a measurement problem that the company's disclosure does nothing to solve. The model's own return framework shows the consequence: with negative two-year-forward earnings the guideline fair multiple is 0.0x and the computed total shareholder return (-1,607% to the 18-month column) has no meaning. The memo's topics #11 and #12 should be answered on enterprise value, fixed-charge coverage and the sum of the parts (the first set's C-83 table, updated for the branches), with EPS shown only to document the gap to consensus.

5. Peer comparison. Flutter and MGM publish currency sensitivities; PENN and Boyd guide property EBITDAR by segment and quarter; Caesars discloses rent coverage and interest by instrument; Bally's Intralot gives building blocks. Bally's is the least traceable company in its cohort on its own disclosure and among the most traceable on third-party disclosure, which is an unusual combination and the reason monitoring (D-05) is more feasible than the communication score suggests.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

F1028th of 28

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: Sentiment risks are severe, amplified by very thin liquidity and the extreme equity gearing (C-76).

  • Company headlines: going-concern updates in the Q3 2026 10-Q; GLPI commentary (its Q3 10-Q of 29 October 2026 already warned); a credit-rating downgrade; CFO appointment news; Pomerantz and other class actions; Chicago political headlines (Axios on graffiti and the timeline, 26 October 2026).
  • Sector: a de-rated European and US gambling sector (Flutter -62%, Entain -39%); UK budget talk of further duties; prediction-market headlines; Bally's Intralot results (27 November 2026) and the Evoke deal; regional gaming revenue prints.
  • Market: the FfF regime is credit-led risk-off, with high-yield yields up 90 basis points and Fragility Double Underweight. Small, complex, geared names are sold first ("aggressively sell small, niche, weird, unproven, risky, complex").
  • Liquidity: average daily value traded is about $1.3m and the float about 20m shares (calculated: about 33% of about 60m shares), so modest selling moves the price far.
  • Precedent: the shares fell 26% on the going-concern disclosure and 8.2% after the Q2 miss.
  • Technical position: price $13.52, above its 50-day ($12.01), 150-day ($12.58) and 200-day ($12.91) averages; up from a 52-week low of $8.44; relative strength at the 25th percentile; technical momentum D. A recovering base after capitulation, vulnerable to a failed refinancing headline.
  • Upside sentiment risk: a Standard General take-private or recapitalisation announcement could move the stock up sharply.

C-61How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?F1828th of 28full answer under 4b Management quality
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

F828th of 28

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: There is no record of guidance being upgraded. The pattern under both the previous and current CEOs is guidance set optimistically and then cut, followed by guidance being abandoned altogether.

  • 2022: revenue and EBITDA ranges cut twice (Atlantic City), and full-year guidance cut on currency.
  • 2023: adjusted EBITDAR guidance cut by $35m at the midpoint and revenue by $100m at the midpoint (Chicago delays). Deutsche Bank said the 2023 guidance was ultimately met.
  • 2024: guidance set below consensus (Deutsche Bank: 3% to 9% below), with revenue landing at the lower end of $2.5bn to $2.7bn.
  • 2025 and 2026: no consolidated guidance.
  • At the subsidiary: the FY2025 target of €435m was delivered at €430.8m (a slight miss), and the CEO conceded the duty delays the business plan by a year.
  • Not a sandbagger: this is the opposite of a management team that guides precisely and conservatively (the Wisdom Library's "sandbagging precision" archetype).

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

F1028th of 28

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: Predominantly misses over every horizon, with the misses concentrated in tougher conditions, which today's are.

  • One year:
  • Q2 2026 EPS of -$2.41 against a -$0.99 consensus (Benzinga), with the shares down 8.2% after hours.
  • Q2 adjusted EBITDA down year on year (-4.0%), against expectations of margin expansion (latest review).
  • Q1 2026 revenue met expectations, but the result relied on a $105.8m sale-leaseback gain.
  • Three years:
  • Consensus EBITDAR missed in all four quarters of 2024; the largest miss was Q4 2024 ($138.3m against $148.1m, -6.62%).
  • Q3 2024 revenue of $630.0m against $637.2m.
  • Six consecutive revenue misses to November 2024 (Zacks).
  • 2024 guidance below consensus; 2023 guidance cut (Deutsche Bank says the cut guidance was met).
  • Five years: in 2022, guidance was cut twice for Atlantic City and once more for currency. That was the most comparable environment (rising rates, inflation).
  • At the subsidiary: FY2025 delivered €430.8m against a €435m target, and pro forma revenue of €1,075m came in against €1,086m expected.
  • Evoke (to be absorbed by the subsidiary): its record is mixed but improving.
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

F1225th of 25

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: The forecasts deserve low weight beyond two to three quarters, and every management-dependent element should be leaned toward delay and shortfall. Bally's record over one, three and five years is of misses and cuts in exactly today's conditions (rising rates, consumer squeeze, development). My base already contains the lean: Chicago a quarter late at about 60% of the analysts' earnings, core casinos flat, B2C below consensus, and interest well above consensus. The residual skew after that lean is still down on cash and on EPS. On EBITDA it is two-sided, with the upside resting on one driver (a Chicago ramp near Deutsche Bank's model) and the downside on several. Weights by horizon: FY2026 EBITDA high (H1 is reported); FY2027 EBITDA medium-low (±15%); FY2027 EPS low (it depends on refinancing terms); FY2028 to FY2030 EPS very low (they depend on which capital-structure branch occurs).

EBITDA after rent Missed consensus EBITDAR in all four quarters of 2024 (Q4 -6.6%); Q2 2026 adjusted EBITDA down 4% when margin expansion was expected FY2027 $459m, 15% below consensus ($541m) Two-sided, about -$80m to +$150m; the upside rests on Chicago alone

Controls and data quality Ineffective disclosure controls (31 March 2026); a material weakness in tax accounting; two late filings in 2026; earlier restatements Tax and below-the-line items held conservatively Restatement risk; lowers confidence in all reported numbers

2. Why lean down rather than simply widen. The misses cluster in conditions like today's (2022: rates and inflation; 2024: consumer softness and development), and they come from structural sources (fixed charges, under-investment, optimism on development) rather than from noise. They also compound, because each miss worsens the financing loop. A symmetric widening would understate the probability of the low outcomes.

3. Where the lean could be wrong.

  • North America Interactive has beaten expectations twice (Q1 and Q2 2026).
  • UK online has proved more resilient than feared: about +13% in July 2026, after the duty.
  • The regional consumer has been more resilient than sentiment suggests: Boyd saw "no material impact" from gasoline prices (Morgan Stanley, 5 October 2026).
  • Standard General has repeatedly put capital in.

These argue against leaning revenue harder than I have. They do not touch the fixed-charge arithmetic.

4. What would raise the weight. Two clean quarters (Q3 and Q4 2026) without one-offs dominating; a refinancing with disclosed terms; a permanent CFO with a capital-markets background; and Chicago opening with disclosed monthly gaming revenue from the Illinois Gaming Board. Each would let the Consumer narrow the FY2027 cone.

For the Consumer: Give my FY2026 numbers high weight, FY2027 EBITDA medium-low weight and FY2027 to FY2030 EPS low weight. Lean any number that depends on management delivery (Chicago date and ramp, the Bally's Intralot guidance, synergies) toward delay and shortfall. The next two tests: Bally's Q3 2026 results (due within days) and Bally's Intralot's Q3 results on 27 November 2026.

C-49What earnings-per-share growth is the company generating, with what revenue growth, and how durable is it?F628th of 28full answer under 4c Financial profile
Group judgement

Financial forecasts

F925th of 25

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts for Bally's are best understood as two models that disagree with each other. The operating model is moderately predictable and only mildly negative. Revenue grows mostly through consolidation and Chicago. Core casinos are flat. North America Interactive turns modestly profitable. The UK subsidiary absorbs the 40% duty with about 65% mitigation. EBITDAR rises from about $731m (FY2026) to about $944m (FY2030). The financing model is the opposite. Rent rises from $248m to $357m, with Chicago's $80m arriving before Chicago's earnings. Net interest rises from $477m to about $665m, because the parent's structural deficit of about $300m to $400m a year is funded with debt at 12% to 13%. EBITDAR therefore never covers rent plus interest by more than about 1.0 times. Adjusted EPS stays around -$8.6 to -$8.9 from FY2027. Free cash flow is negative every year. Attributable book equity erodes to about zero by 2030.

The insight for the Consumer is that the forecast differences from the past rest on few drivers, and the two that matter most are dated: rent steps, and the refinancing that must happen by 31 March 2027. That makes the next 18 months' operating numbers easier to predict than usual for a company this complex. The equity outcome is harder to predict, because it depends on terms that Standard General, the lenders and GLPI will set. Against consensus, my FY2027 numbers are about 4% lower on revenue, 15% lower on EBITDA after rent, $118m higher on interest and $3.40 a share worse on EPS. Most of that gap comes from two items consensus appears not to model: Chicago's rent arriving at opening, and the repricing of debt. Price, cost-cutting and the positive feedback loops are small next to the fixed-charge arithmetic. The positives that do exist (UK consolidation, North America Interactive, the Rhode Island floor) sit largely in the 58%-owned subsidiary or are too small to change the direction.

P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

F625th of 25

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: Below operating profit, every item works against shareholders, and the gap between adjusted and reported results stays wide. Net interest rises from $401m (FY2025) and $477m (FY2026) to $582m (FY2027) and about $665m by FY2030 in a debt-funded bridge. The tax charge is permanently positive despite losses ($25m to $50m a year), because profits are taxed in the UK, Gibraltar, Greece and Turkey, while US losses carry a full valuation allowance. Minorities currently cushion losses by about $20m a year, because Bally's Intralot is loss-making at the net level, but that fades as it de-levers. "One-off" costs recur at $40m to $110m a year. The share count is already 25% above the FactSet figure, from penny warrants, and the realistic dilution risk is a recapitalisation, not awards. No dividends or buybacks are plausible before FY2030. The result: adjusted EPS of about -$6.3 (FY2026), -$8.8 (FY2027) and about -$8.6 to -$8.9 (FY2028 to FY2030). Reported EPS will be worse by $0.5 to $3 a share in any year with transaction, Star or extinguishment items. Free cash flow is negative in every year of my base: -$1,025m in FY2026 (including the $502m New York licence), -$672m in FY2027, and about -$470m to -$490m a year after that, including the Bronx.

Margins/growth vs. past

6 answers

C-53How high are gross and operating profit margins, and how are they trending?F1528th of 28full answer under 4c Financial profile
C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

F3028th of 28

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: Improvement would come from a few big, lumpy areas, each with dated execution risk, and much of the gain accrues to others. The scope is congruent with the strategy, but the strategy is not funded.

  • Chicago permanent casino: about $30m to $110m more EBITDA after rent at stabilisation, 2028 at the earliest (C-50). Precedents: PENN's Aurora (volumes nearly doubled), Joliet (slot volumes up 1.4 times) and Red Rock's Durango (returns over 20%). Counter-precedents: Fitch calls Chicago saturated and expects a slow ramp, and the temporary casino has underperformed.
  • North America Interactive: $12m to $15m of extra EBITDA by late 2027 if sustained (latest review), with 50% shared with Bally's Intralot once breakeven is reached.
  • Bally's Intralot: €41m of synergies (€15m in FY2026, €25m in FY2027); completing the UK mitigation; Evoke's £180m of synergies (75% within twelve months). Evoke delivered £150m against a £100m target on William Hill, and Lottomatica's €119m beat expectations by more than 50%, so this is achievable. Bally's share is 58% or less.
  • Asset monetisation: the Tropicana land and the Star stake. Proceeds are uncertain, and the use is debt reduction.
  • Refinancing: only if credit improves. The latest review's "change my mind" trigger is $500m or more refinanced below a 10% blended cost, with maturities extended past 2029.
  • Peer precedent of a full recovery from this starting leverage without a restructuring: rare. Caesars' operating company and Station Casinos both restructured.
C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

F2826th of 28

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: Yes. Underneath acquisition-inflated headline growth, the core engine has decelerated. Casinos & Resorts organic growth is running at about 2%, below Bally's own normal of about 3% to 5% in benign years, and its input force (US regional consumer gaming spend) is itself slowing. The one accelerating engine, UK online, is about to be taxed through generosity cuts.

  • Headline: consolidated revenue growth of +28.3% in Q1 and +20.5% in Q2 2026 is acquisition- and consolidation-driven.
  • Casinos & Resorts: +8.1% in Q1 2026, helped by Queen properties not yet in the prior-year base until 7 February 2025 and by the landside moves; +2.0% in Q2.
  • The market: September regional gaming revenue +2.5%, down from mid-single digits.
  • North America Interactive: +35.9% in Q1, then +16.9% in Q2. Industry iGaming slowed from 16% to 13%.
  • UK online: accelerated from +10.5% to +11.6% in constant currency, with the generosity cuts still to come.
  • Bally's Intralot B2B: legacy lottery revenue -10.1% reported (-6.5% in constant currency) in H1 2026; Ohio exits mid-2027.
  • Consensus revenue: +8% a year in FY2027 to FY2028, which needs Chicago's step-up plus organic growth. That is plausible only if Chicago ramps on time.
C-67

How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

F1528th of 28

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: Consensus already expects losses and negative free cash flow, so it is not bullish. But it is still too high in three specific places: FY2027 EBITDA growth (+11.7%) assumes Chicago adds net of its new rent in its first partial year; interest appears to be held roughly flat at about $450m to $470m a year despite refinancing at today's spreads and the new Bronx loan; and FY2028 positive free cash flow (+$44m) assumes no further development spending at the parent. Consensus also does not include Evoke, which will change the consolidated perimeter if completed.

  • FY2026 revenue: credible. H1 was $1,548m, and H2 of about $1,614m is achievable with Sam's Town and a full Intralot year.
  • FY2026 EBITDA: broadly credible, with a modest downside (H2 implied at $237m against H1's $247m).
  • FY2027 EBITDA: at risk by roughly 5% to 15%. Chicago's rent starts at opening, before stabilisation (C-75). The UK betting duty arrives in April 2027 and the Ohio exit (about -$23m a year at 100%) in mid-2027.
  • FY2027 EPS: at risk by about $0.5 to $1.5 a share from interest alone (C-76).
  • FY2028: depends on Chicago stabilising and refinancing on reasonable terms; low credibility.
  • Book value per share: falling 29% over two years even on consensus, so the equity cushion shrinks.

P-09

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

F1025th of 25

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: The next four years look like a larger, lower-margin, more indebted version of the last four. Revenue is about 45% higher than FY2025 by FY2030 (consolidation of Bally's Intralot's lottery business, Chicago, Sam's Town), but organic growth slows to about 1% in the core casinos. EBITDA after rent falls in FY2027 and is no higher in FY2028 than in FY2026. It recovers only to about $590m by FY2030 (15.4% margin), below FY2024's 20% margin. The reason is that rent rises by about $100m and the UK duty permanently removes 7 to 8 points of B2C margin. Interest rises faster than EBITDA, so losses widen rather than narrow. My adjusted EPS of -$8.6 to -$8.9 in FY2027 to FY2030 is worse than consensus and shows no trend to profit. Free cash flow stays negative throughout. The net effect of the forces in this set is negative and concentrated in two places: the fixed-charge line (rent plus interest) and the timing and ramp of Chicago.

2. The bridge of EBITDA after rent, FY2026 to FY2028 (my base, USD m): FY2026 $483m.

  • Core casinos: -$6m (flat revenue, margin -50bp over two years)
  • Sam's Town: +$9m (full year, partial recovery)
  • Chicago EBITDAR: +$75m (temporary $13m to permanent $88m)
  • North America Interactive: +$16m
  • B2C: +$15m (mitigation, synergies, +3% to +4% revenue)
  • B2B: -$10m (Ohio exit, repricing)
  • Corporate: +$2m
  • Rent: -$98m (Chicago -$81m; escalators -$17m)

The result is FY2028 $486m. Chicago's EBITDAR gain (+$75m) is smaller than its rent (-$81m) through FY2028. Everything else nets to about +$9m.

3. The forecast differences, concentrated or diffuse. The difference between FY2025 and FY2028 rests on four drivers that account for nearly all the change. Two are dated and contractual: rent steps (+$156m since FY2025, including Lincoln and Chicago) and refinancing (+$190m of interest since FY2025). One is policy: the UK duty, about $110m to $140m gross a year, of which roughly 65% is mitigated. One is execution: Chicago's ramp. The first three are near-certain in direction and size; the fourth sets the width of the cone. That concentration helps the Consumer's predictability work: the drivers are few and observable. It also means the forecast leans on a single execution variable (Chicago) and a single financing variable (the terms reached by 31 March 2027).

4. The Evoke overlay (not in my base; completion is likely in Q4 2026 to Q2 2027). Shareholders approved with 99.63%, and the cash alternative is capped at £117.1m. My base excludes Evoke to keep it comparable with consensus, which also excludes it, and because the share-or-cash election and the timing are unknown. On completion around 1 April 2027 (my inference, low confidence):

Revenue added (Evoke revenue about £1.75bn a year) about +1,750 about +2,350 about +2,400

D&A added, including acquisition amortisation about -130 about -180 about -180

Evoke roughly doubles the subsidiary, raises consolidated revenue to about $5.9bn in FY2028 and lifts the consolidated EBITDA margin about 1.5 points. It adds about $2.4bn of ring-fenced debt and changes Bally's EPS by little. For Bally's equity, its importance is the value and leverage of the 51% to 58% stake, not earnings. Evoke's July 2028 maturity sits inside the four-year window and is a refinancing risk at the subsidiary.

For the Consumer: The model's line items move in one direction for one reason. Revenue grows, but fixed charges grow faster, so profit and cash fall against FY2022 to FY2025 levels before slowly stabilising. Concentrate predictability testing on the four drivers above. Treat the Evoke overlay as a reporting event that will make reported growth jump in FY2027 without changing the equity case much.

Memo topic 7, : the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

F2515th of 15

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The question the fourth set asks is whether current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect what we think will happen, primarily over 18 months. They do not, and the direction of the gap is the wrong way for an investment case in the common equity. Guidance is absent at the parent and above the run-rate at the subsidiary, with the next move more likely a rebase than a raise (I-01). Consensus is already negative on EPS and free cash flow, but still too high on FY2027 EBITDA by about 15% because it does not carry Chicago's rent from opening, too low on interest by about $120m because it does not carry the refinancing or the WhiteHawk loan, and too high on FY2028 by more; the revisions to come are cuts on the lines that matter and mechanical rises on the lines that do not (I-02). The narrative knows the headline, not the arithmetic: the parent's own fixed-charge gap, the rent-before-earnings timing at Chicago, the sponsor's limited capital and the covenant mechanics are articulated by one credit desk and by the landlord's filings and by nobody on the equity side; what the equity narrative does carry is management's Chicago number, which is above ours (I-03). The price itself shows what is reflected: an option on Standard General and Chicago, rallied 60% on a 13% bridge loan, priced between the sponsor's last two bids, moving with financing headlines and not with its sector.

Three steers for the memo. First, the configuration the framework looks for in topic #4 and topic #7 (conservative guidance, a record of raising, estimates below what the forecasts will deliver) is absent on every count; the only expectations that sit below our forecasts are the sell-side's EPS on some brokers' bases and the free cash flow consensus for FY2026, and neither is a line the price trades on. Second, the information that will move guidance, estimates and narrative over the next six months is dated and mostly negative in expectation (the Q3 liquidity test, the 27 November guidance question, the 10 December Chicago deadline, the March 2027 refinancing), while the information that could move them positively (a sponsor bid, a benign exchange of the unsecured notes, a strong Chicago opening) is lumpy, binary and mostly later. Third, the perimeter change from Evoke will produce a false positive in every revision statistic in 2027 and the memo should say so before it happens. The favourable reading of the gap, that the narrative is so bleak that any survival re-rates the stock, is real and explains the rally since September, but it is a trade on a financing headline against a sponsor's incentives, not an investment case in which the forecasts lead expectations upward. On the fourth set's question, the gap between our assessment and what expectations already reflect is unfavourable in the primary window and unfavourable on the Chicago upside beyond it, with a narrow favourable strip on the credit and on event-driven survival.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

F2515th of 15

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement: Bally's has no financial guidance to raise, meet or reduce, and is unlikely to issue any within 18 months (I put the probability at about 20%, and only after a refinancing or amendment removes the going-concern doubt, which makes guidance in 2027 more plausible than guidance in 2026). The guidance that exists in the group is Bally's Intralot's, and the balance of probability is that it is reduced: I put about 60% on the FY2026 adjusted EBITDA guidance of about €422m being rebased, most likely to the high €390s or low €400s, either at the Q3 results on 27 November 2026 or with the FY2026 results in March 2027; about 30% on it being nominally kept and delivered with a small miss, as FY2025 was (€430.8m against €435m); and about 10% on a beat, which would need the UK to keep growing at the 12% to 14% the scaled peers printed in Q3 while the generosity cuts land. The FY2027 pro forma target of €454m is more likely to be replaced than met: Evoke's completion changes the perimeter, and a combined-group target (management has quoted about €856m of pro forma 2025 EBITDA for the enlarged group) will supersede it, which lets management retire €454m without visibly cutting it. The informal milestones that stand in for Bally's own guidance (Chicago open in H1 2027; "development liquidity ensured"; a "route to profitability") will be met, missed and met respectively on my base: Chicago is more likely to open in Q3 2027 than by June (I put 30% on an opening by 30 June 2027, 40% on Q3, 30% on Q4 or later), the Bronx's liquidity is ensured only until the WhiteHawk maturity in April 2028, and North America Interactive's profitability is the one milestone the record supports. The gap between our assessment and what guidance reflects is therefore unfavourable: the only numbers management has put its name to are above what the run-rate supports, and the milestones that matter most for the parent are the ones most likely to slip.

1. Bally's own guidance: why there is none and what would bring it back. The last formal guidance was for FY2024 (set below consensus, delivered at the low end). Guidance was cut twice in 2022 and once in 2023, and abandoned for 2025 and 2026. The stated reason in the digests (the take-private) is stale; the operative reason is that management cannot guide to a year it does not project funding ("does not project meeting liquidity maintenance... without additional financing"). Guidance returns only if (a) a bridge or recapitalisation is signed, (b) the FY2026 10-K carries no going-concern qualification, and (c) Chicago's opening gives the company a story to guide to. The sequence makes guidance possible with the Q1 2027 results in May 2027 at the earliest, and more likely with the Q2 2027 results in August 2027 if Chicago has opened. If the capital-structure event is a recapitalisation or take-private, public guidance becomes moot. I would not count on any guidance before the memo's 18-month horizon ends, and I would read its reinstatement, if it comes, as a signal that the lenders' terms have been settled rather than as a forecast.

2. Bally's Intralot's FY2026 guidance: the arithmetic of a rebase. H1 2026 adjusted EBITDA was €184.8m. Reaching €422m needs H2 of about €237m, 28% above H1. The second half is seasonally stronger for lottery (jackpots) and for UK online (Q4), but H2 carries two quarters of the 40% duty (a gross hit of about €34m a quarter, of which 65% was offset in Q2, the first quarter, and which Deutsche Bank doubts can be sustained as "none of the other UK-listed operators are confident of mitigating 35% in the first year"), the B2B segment's revenue fell 10.1% in H1, and the Interactive outlook of €280m to €295m plus a lottery run-rate of about €104m sums to €384m to €399m. Management said in May it was "tracking fully in line". The drivers of each outcome:

  • Reduced (60%): Q3 UK growth slows below mid-single digits as the generosity cuts bite, or lottery jackpots do not recover, or the Interactive figure lands in its range and the lottery does not fill the gap. The most likely form is a rebase to "around €400m" presented alongside Evoke's completion and a combined outlook, which blunts the headline.
  • Met with a small miss (30%): UK growth holds near 10% (the scaled peers' Q3 prints are the best argument for this), the €15m of 2026 synergies land, and management reports a figure within 2% of €422m, as it did for FY2025.
  • Raised or beaten (10%): UK growth stays at 12% to 14% with lower generosity, which would show that the recreational, bingo-led base is less price-sensitive than any peer's, and sub-scale rivals' exits accelerate share gains. Either way, this is the subsidiary's number; Bally's consolidates the EBITDAR on a different basis (our $369m of B2C plus B2B EBITDAR in FY2026, about €315m) and its minority takes 42%, so a €20m rebase is worth about $14m of consolidated EBITDAR and about $0.13 a share after minorities. The signal matters more than the size: a rebase would be the first cut to any number in the group since 2023 and would confirm the pattern (C-70) just as the parent's refinancing is being negotiated.

3. The 18-month calendar of guidance-like events and the likely outcome at each.

4. Secondarily, 18 months to three years out. The only mid-term targets are the subsidiary's: 2.5x leverage "within two years" (Deutsche Bank does not see it before FY2029; Evoke takes pro forma leverage to about 4.5x, so this will be restated, not met), £180m of Evoke synergies by year two (achievable on Evoke's own William Hill record of £150m against a £100m target, and the Lottomatica precedent of upgraded synergy targets, but Deutsche Bank warns they may only offset the UK duty), and the Interactive EBITDA range. At the parent, the only three-year ambitions are Chicago at $250m of EBITDAR (management's figure; our stabilised $150m; Deutsche Bank's 2027 figure $214m before the hotel pause), a 2030 Bronx opening (the capital stack is unresolved beyond pre-construction) and a Las Vegas development with no capital. I would expect the $250m Chicago figure to be quietly replaced by a lower run-rate once monthly receipts are public, the Bronx date to hold while the equity partner is unnamed, and no new mid-term targets to be set until the capital structure is settled. Net, the 18-to-36-month ambitions are more likely to be reduced or replaced than met, and none is likely to be raised.

5. What this means against our assessment. Our base case is below the subsidiary's guidance on the subsidiary's own basis, assumes Chicago opens a quarter after the stated window, and assumes the Bronx is funded with 13% debt until an unnamed partner appears. Guidance, such as it is, therefore sits above our assessment everywhere it exists, and the probable direction of travel at the next events is down toward us, not up away from us. That is the worst configuration for topic #4 of the memo (conservative guidance, a record of raising) and for topic #7 (scope for upgrades): there is no conservatism to unwind and no track record of raising to extrapolate.

Sellside sales

I-02

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

F2215th of 15

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: Consensus for FY2027 is more likely to be cut than held, and far more likely to be cut than raised, on the two lines that matter: EBITDA (I put about 70% on the FY2027 median falling by at least 10% from $541m by the Q2 2027 results, toward our $459m) and EPS (about 75% on the median falling further from -$5.41, driven by the interest line once the refinancing terms are known). Revenue consensus will look stable or even rise, which is a trap: the perimeter changes (Evoke's consolidation at the subsidiary, about $1.75bn of annualised revenue, and Sam's Town) will mechanically lift reported sales and EBITDA estimates without changing the parent's claim, so the dashboard's revision statistics could turn positive on sales and EBITDA in 2027 for reasons that have nothing to do with the investment case. Breadth cannot deteriorate further in the ordinary sense (five brokers, all Hold or Sell, a 0% buy share, a $10.50 mean target 22% below the price) and will not improve without a capital-structure event; the realistic breadth risk is attrition of coverage (Deutsche Bank and Jefferies have already dropped equity coverage; three of the five remaining estimates are dated 17 August 2026) rather than further downgrades. The pattern of revisions since August (EPS -24% over three months with sales unchanged) shows the brokers are already cutting below the line without touching the operating lines; the next leg is the operating line, when Chicago's rent-at-opening and a later opening are modelled. For 18 months to three years out, FY2028 consensus (two brokers on EBITDA at $630m; free cash flow +$44m; EPS -$5.26) rests on Chicago stabilising, interest flat and no Bronx spending, and I expect it to be cut substantially; the one-month "improvement" in the FY2028 mean (-23.7% less negative) is a coverage artefact from a single broker's update, not a trend.

1. Where consensus sits against our base, and which lines will move.

2. The timetable of likely revisions.

  • Q3 2026 results (about 10 November 2026). The three stale brokers update. Operating lines broadly held (Q3 revenue about $795m on our base, consolidation-led growth of about 20%; EBITDA about $125m on the company's basis); EPS cut for the WhiteHawk interest and any Q3 one-offs; targets trimmed toward the $7 to $10 range if the liquidity disclosure is weak. Net: EPS down, EBITDA flat, sales flat.
  • Bally's Intralot Q3 (27 November 2026). If the €422m is rebased, the brokers covering Bally's trim B2C EBITDAR by $10m to $15m; a UK growth print below 6% would prompt a larger cut to FY2027 B2C. Net: EBITDA slightly down.
  • FY2026 results and the refinancing (late February to March 2027). The largest single revision event: interest re-based to the signed terms (our $582m against the implied $464m); the Chicago opening date set; Evoke's consolidation modelled by some brokers and not others, which will widen the dispersion of revenue and EBITDA estimates and make the median unreliable for a quarter or two. Net: EPS down sharply; EBITDA down underlying, up headline where Evoke is consolidated.
  • Q1 and Q2 2027 (May and August 2027). Chicago's rent and ramp enter the models as actuals; the FY2027 EBITDA median converges toward the mid-$400s underlying. If Chicago opens strongly (Deutsche Bank-like receipts), this is the one window in which EBITDA consensus could rise; I give that about 20%.
  • Q3 2027 onward. The FY2028 numbers are rebuilt from Chicago's first quarters and the refinanced interest; the free-cash-flow line goes from +$44m to negative.

3. Breadth and the dashboard's momentum score. The buy share is 0% and cannot fall; the revision-momentum score is -8. Two things could move breadth: a capital-structure event that removes the going-concern doubt (a bridge or recapitalisation would likely bring one or two upgrades to Buy from Hold on "survival", because the sell-side's stated reason for Hold is leverage, not operations), or a sponsor bid (ratings would move to the bid price). Neither is an earnings-driven upgrade. Conversely, coverage attrition is the more probable breadth change: with Barclays at Sell and no estimate update since 17 August, and the dropped coverage at Deutsche Bank (October 2024) and Jefferies (May 2025), the next change may be four brokers rather than five. For the memo's topic #8 (momentum in sell-side upgrades), there is none and none is in prospect from fundamentals; for topic #7 (scope for upgrades over 6 to 12 months), the scope is on the downside for EBITDA and EPS and on the upside only for the perimeter.

4. The perimeter trap, stated for the memo. When Evoke completes (late 2026 or early 2027), Bally's consolidated revenue rises by about 55% and consolidated EBITDA by about 50% at the subsidiary level, while Bally's stake in Bally's Intralot falls from 58% to about 51% in an all-share outcome and the subsidiary's leverage rises to about 4.5x. Any screen that reads sales or EBITDA revisions as upgrades (the dashboard's revision data; the "sell-side momentum" rating) will register a large positive move that is entirely consolidation. The memo should state this in advance and judge revisions on parent-level or per-share lines, or on EBITDA after rent and after minorities, which is the only line that will not be flattered.

5. Peers' read-across, for calibration. PENN raised retail guidance twice in 2026 and Deutsche Bank upgraded it to Buy in October on deleveraging; Boyd was upgraded by Morgan Stanley in October on a sell-off; Caesars' Q3 was flat and it is tethered to the Fertitta deal price; Red Rock grew revenue 2.8% in Q3. Regional demand is holding, which is why I do not expect the operating lines of Bally's consensus to be cut for demand reasons. UK online peers (Entain, Flutter, Rank) printed double-digit growth in Q3 and the Machine Games Duty landed at 25% rather than 40%, a "clearing event" for the sector; the UK read-across is positive for Bally's Intralot's revenue and neutral to negative for its guidance, because its mitigation claim (65%) is the sector's highest. Evoke's consensus around its 2025 refinancing was cut 15% to 20% as higher interest was modelled; that is the closest precedent for what will happen to Bally's EPS after its own refinancing.

6. Secondarily, 18 months to three years out. FY2028 consensus EBITDA of $630m (two brokers) against our $486m, consensus EBIT of $177m against our $70m, and consensus free cash flow of +$44m against our -$481m. The FY2028 estimates assume Chicago at or near stabilisation in its second year, interest flat, and no development spending at the parent; each assumption is more likely to be cut than confirmed. I put about 75% on FY2028 EBITDA consensus being lower by the time it becomes the "next year" (early 2028) and about 85% on FY2028 free cash flow consensus turning negative. The exception is again the perimeter: a consolidated Evoke makes the FY2028 headline EBITDA number larger than $630m even as the parent's own EBITDA is cut. The Consumer should keep two series: headline (consolidated, including Evoke) and parent look-through.

11d

Current multiple

–not yet ratedMemo topic 11, : the Rater's score1 answer
D-0732
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

F3215th of 15

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: Almost entirely idiosyncratically. Over the latest six-month window the share price's correlation with the equal-weighted basket of its thirteen dashboard peers is 0.08, its beta to the basket 0.45 and the share of its daily variance the basket explains 1%; the remaining 99% is the stock's own. Over the 24 month-end windows the explained share has never exceeded 10% (June 2026) and was 0% to 2% in sixteen of them. Even Bally's Intralot, the 58%-owned subsidiary that is the group's best asset, explains only 5% of the parent's moves (correlation 0.23). The multiple, so far as one exists, is therefore company-driven: the price is set by the capital-structure narrative (the going-concern note took the shares down 26% to the $8.44 low; the WhiteHawk financing and GLPI's continued funding took them back up about 60% to $13.52), not by the sector's de-rating (Flutter -62%, Entain -39%, Betsson -34% over twelve months against Bally's -27%). For predictability this is a mixed reading. The positive is that the forecasts and the price are not hostage to the sector's fiscal and prediction-market narrative, so a sector recovery would not help and a further sector fall would not hurt much; what moves the price is the handful of dated company events in D-04, which we can at least name. The negative is that those events are binary and the stock is thin (about 120,000 shares or $1.3m to $1.6m a day; a float of about 20m shares), so the price path is a sequence of gaps on headlines rather than a drift that fundamentals can be read into. A multiple that the market sets on the company's own survival odds is less predictable than one set by the industry's cycle.

1. The co-movement evidence.

Window end Correlation with basket Beta R-squared What was happening
January 2025 0.24 0.96 6% Pre-merger; the shares pinned near the $18.25 deal price
February to December 2025 -0.15 to +0.05 -1.25 to +0.18 0% to 2% The merger completes (7 February 2025); the Intralot transaction; the shares trade on deal and financing news
April to July 2026 0.29 to 0.32 0.92 to 1.04 8% to 10% The only period of sector linkage: the UK duty (1 April 2026), prediction-market headlines and the sector's de-rating coincided with Bally's Q1 and Q2 results
August to November 2026 0.24 falling to 0.08 1.09 falling to 0.45 6% falling to 1% The going-concern note (14 August), the CFO's exit (3 September), the WhiteHawk financing (14 September and 2 October), GLPI's warning (29 October): company events swamp the sector

Six-month returns are flat for Bally's and -1.0% for the basket, which hides a round trip of roughly -40% and +60% inside the window for Bally's. Correlations with individual peers are all between -0.11 and +0.23; the two highest are Bally's Intralot (0.23) and Flutter (0.22).

2. What this says about the multiple. The dashboard holds no price-to-earnings reading because earnings are negative; the only multiple that can be computed is enterprise value to EBITDA, about 14x FY2026 consensus on the FactSet enterprise value of $6.8bn (which includes $1.5bn of minorities and FactSet's lease-inclusive debt), against regional-casino references of 6x to 8x (Deutsche Bank's 6.0x domestic and 7.0x interactive; Jefferies' 6.8x blended; Standard General's 2024 bid at 7.8x; Caesars-Fertitta at 7.5x). That 14x is not a valuation the market is paying for Bally's earnings; it is the arithmetic of a small equity sitting under a large, fixed enterprise value. The equity's own value ($852m market capitalisation) is less than the market value of the 58% Bally's Intralot stake (about $1.1bn to $1.4bn at €1.12), so the market assigns a negative value to the parent's casinos net of parent debt and a positive value to the Standard General and Chicago options. The co-movement data confirm that reading: the stock does not trade as a claim on gambling earnings; it trades as an option whose value changes with financing headlines. The first set's C-83 framing (option value plus the stake plus sponsor expectation) is therefore right, and I would add one observation: the price has settled between Standard General's 2024 bid ($15) and the post-going-concern low ($8.44), which suggests the market is anchoring on the sponsor's past prices as a reference for a future one.

3. Why the price moves alone, and whether that persists. Three structural reasons: a controlled company with a third of the shares in public hands and average daily value traded near $1.3m, so a single institutional order moves the price; a going-concern company whose news flow is financing, litigation and municipal politics rather than gaming revenue; and a conglomerate (US casinos, US online, UK online, global lottery, Australian stake) whose peers are each a poor match. None of the three changes in 18 months. The only scenario in which the stock would re-couple with its sector is branch E (a clean refinancing) or a successful Chicago ramp that turns the narrative from solvency to earnings; I give that a small weight within the window. Expect the idiosyncratic share to stay above 90%.

4. For the memo's topics #10 and #11. The lifecycle reading from the technicals (momentum D; price above the 50-, 150- and 200-day averages; the 200-day rising in nine of twelve months; relative strength in the 25th percentile; a 60% recovery from the capitulation low) is "bottoming after capitulation on a financing bridge", not "turning up on fundamentals". The fair multiple cannot be set from a price-to-earnings range because the company has no positive earnings in its history since FY2019 on a reported basis and none forecast; it should be set as a sum of the parts at trough enterprise-value multiples for the operating business (6x to 7x parent EBITDA after rent) plus the stake at market, with the sponsor option valued explicitly and separately, because that is what the co-movement data say the market is doing.

Outside the eleven ratings

Answers not placed under any of the eleven ratings

The table the eleven ratings page uses places these nowhere; each is shown with the reason the table records.

Why it is not placed It asks whether the stock fits the sector's historical patterns of share price and financial outperformance, a cross-check across many topics rather than one of the eleven.

C-84

What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and the empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry have typically generated share price, financial and operational outperformance, and underperformance, and to what extent does this company fit those patterns?

F1528th of 28

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

The Investment Analyst's heading What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and the empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry have outperformed and underperformed, and how far does this company fit?

Judgement: The precedents are clear, and Bally's fits almost every underperformance pattern and almost none of the outperformance patterns. The one outperformance pattern it could still fit (a successful flagship opening in a protected market) is undercut by rent and leverage, which divert most of the gain to the landlord and lenders.

  • Outperformance patterns: 1. Owned real estate, low leverage and steady buybacks through the cycle (Boyd at 2.5x net debt with 90% of properties owned; Red Rock beating consensus EBITDA for three years). 2. Well-executed relocations and new builds in protected markets (Red Rock's Durango, over 20% returns; PENN's Joliet and Aurora; Bally's own Baton Rouge). 3. Separating property from operations at high multiples, which created value once (GLPI in 2013, VICI in 2017) but left the operators with fixed rent. 4. Scaled online platforms with proprietary technology and low marketing intensity (Lottomatica's online margin from 21% to 57.9%; Gamesys under Reeves: revenue from £340m to £709m between 2018 and 2020, leverage from 3.8x to 1.1x).
  • Underperformance patterns: 1. Lease-adjusted leverage above about 7x entering a downturn or a development phase, which historically precedes restructuring: Station Casinos (Chapter 11 in 2009 after an LBO plus development); Caesars Entertainment Operating Co (Chapter 11 in 2015); Revel (Atlantic City, 2012 and 2014); Tropicana Entertainment (2008). Bally's is at 7.3x to 14x. 2. Debt-funded diversification into US online: Bally's own Gamesys and North America Interactive impairments; PENN's Barstool and ESPN write-offs. 3. Large urban projects with cost overruns and cannibalisation (Revel; Atlantic City in general). 4. Controlled companies with conflicted transactions (the governance discount).
  • Sector rule of thumb (sector digest): "taxes, licences and bans move these share prices more than demand does". Bally's is exposed on three fronts: the UK duty, the Chicago video-terminal decision and New York licensing.
  • Rule of thumb (Wisdom Library): in a credit-led bear market the cleanest case in the sector wins; Bally's is the least clean.

Why it is not placed The table holds this question in reserve for Riskiness sub-items that are not yet built, so it is not yet placed.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

F528th of 28

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: On fundamentals, the common equity has little or no value at a fair trough multiple. Its current $852m market value is option value on Chicago and the other developments, plus the value of the Bally's Intralot stake, plus the expectation of support or a buyout by Standard General. In a survival case with dilution, a fair trough is about $5 to $8 a share (37% to 63% below $13.52). In a restructuring, it is about $0 to $2 (85% to 100% below).

Why it is not placed It rates the staircase of news the market can look forward to, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or the catalysts as such.

Group judgement

Stock market narrative

F2028th of 28

Layer 1 · Stock market narrative

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The market narrative is "distressed, controlled, complex": a going-concern warning, a covenant cliff, no guidance, no buy ratings and a sector the market is selling. The future staircase has one big operating step (Chicago, spring 2027), whose benefit to earnings after rent arrives only in 2028 and is modest. Before that sit a set of dated negatives (UK duty steps, Ohio and Illinois at the subsidiary, Chicago's rent and the video terminals) and one binary financing step by 31 March 2027. The most likely near-term "positive novelty" is therefore a capital-structure event led by Standard General, whose value to minority holders depends on terms that will be set by the majority holder.

For the Consumer: The forecasts over the next 18 months should be framed around the March 2027 waiver deadline and the Chicago opening, with operating improvements treated as secondary to, and conditional on, the financing outcome.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the earlier answers agree with each other, which is a check on the analysis rather than on the company.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

–62

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: The two sets are highly congruent in direction and mechanism, and the AlphaSense evidence supports them where they agree and resolves most of the places where they differed. Both sets reach the same central finding by different routes: the operating business is stable and mildly growing, the capital structure consumes all of it, and the outcome for equity is set by financing, not operations. The first set reached it qualitatively (fixed charges of rent plus interest exceed parent EBITDAR; Standard General controls the capital-structure decision); the second set quantified it (EBITDAR covers rent plus interest about 1.0 times in FY2026 and less than once from FY2027; a parent funding need of $300m to $400m a year). There are five places where the sets jar or where one was wrong, and in each case I can say which is right. None reverses a conclusion. The one genuine unresolved tension is between the first set's confidence that Standard General can and will support the equity and the new evidence on Standard General's own capital, which lowers the odds of a benign sponsor rescue. For predictability this congruence is a positive: the forecasts rest on one coherent story that the evidence keeps confirming, so the Consumer is not being asked to reconcile two different models of the company.

1. Where the sets support each other, and the evidence now confirms them.

Theme First set Second set What the AlphaSense reports add
Fixed charges exceed parent earnings Parent EBITDAR about $355m against rent about $254m and interest about $300m (an inference from the segment tables) Parent EBITDAR $362m (FY2026), rent $248m, parent cash interest about $245m; funding need $300m to $400m a year including capex Deutsche Bank's credit model (January 2025) had standalone cash rent of $303m and interest of $290m by 2026, and end-2025 fixed charges of $466m with 1.4x coverage; the report on parent cash flows finds no parent-only disclosure and confirms the structural shortfall
The ring-fence Cash reaches the parent only through dividends and intercompany charges Dividends held at about $20m a year Confirmed: the €30m dividend (€17.8m to Bally's) paid 27 July 2026; a restricted-payments basket built from 50% of net income plus €40m; no cross-default to the parent's covenants
Chicago is EBITDA-dilutive at opening Chicago brings rent with it; the value accrues first to GLPI Year-one net effect on EBITDA after rent about -$28m; consensus FY2027 EBITDA needs Chicago to earn above its rent from the first quarter Rent on GLPI's advances starts as advances are made (8.5% on $640.7m already funded, $70.5m of deferred rental income); Deutsche Bank's own base case first-year EBITDAR ($160m) assumed 500 rooms that are now paused; the temporary casino ran at about a quarter of its $50m target
