The UK business is the group's best asset and is ring-fenced Number two in UK online casino; 11% marketing intensity; 58% owned B2C margin recovers to 29% to 30%; UK growth to mid-single digits 65% mitigation of the Q2 duty hit is the best in the sector (Evoke over 50%, Entain targets over 50% from 2027); Q3 2026 peer prints (Entain +14%, Rank +12%) show the UK market was still growing strongly in the quarter

2. Where the sets jar, and how I resolve each.

  • Chicago's incremental rent. The first set used $100m to $132m; the second set $80m. The AlphaSense Chicago report confirms the second set: the $20m land rent has been paid since September 2024 and sits in today's rent, and Jefferies' $132.1m is the total of all Chicago rent. The second set is right.
  • The 11% notes. The first set carried them as outstanding; they were redeemed in October 2025. The second set is right; the first set's interest build was nonetheless anchored on actuals, so no forecast was affected.
  • The intercompany payable. The first set treated the rise in Bally's Intralot's payables to Bally's as the parent extracting cash (C-86). It was the accrued dividend. The first set's risk list should drop that item; the broader point (the parent will want the subsidiary's cash) stands, but the evidence of it happening is weaker than the first set said.
  • Chicago as a "positive deleveraging loop" (first set C-20, C-47, C-22) against the second set's "balancing until FY2029". The second set explicitly disagreed and I side with it: Chicago improves parent EBITDA after rent by only about $65m between FY2027 and FY2030 on the base ramp, against about $110m of added interest from debt-funded deficits over the same period. The first set's score of 42 for C-20 was the most generous in either set and I would now put it in the low 30s.
  • The parent deficit. The first set's "about $200m a year before capex" and the second set's "$300m to $400m after capex" are the same number on different definitions (the second adds about $120m of maintenance and own-development capex and nets the $20m dividend). No disagreement in substance.

3. The one tension the evidence has opened rather than closed. The first set treated Standard General as a credible backstop ("a $18.25 deal, executive control... makes a support or recapitalisation plausible") and the second set gave a Standard General-backed equity raise of about $500m a 20% weight. The Standard General report shows discretionary assets under management of $647m and a pattern of using third-party capital for every large transaction (Apollo's $500m of 11% notes for the Queen merger; HPS warrants at MediaCo; Apollo for the Tegna bid). A $500m cheque is therefore not Standard General's to write alone, and any rescue will carry a third party's return requirement, most likely as structured capital senior to the common equity. That is consistent with the first set's warning that terms would favour the majority holder, but it shifts the weight from "support at a price" towards "restructuring on sponsor terms". My branch weights in Note 6 reflect that. This is a refinement of the earlier sets, not a contradiction.

4. A second, smaller tension: the Rhode Island moat. The first set scored Rhode Island as Bally's strongest and most durable moat (C-41) and noted the regulatory agreement's 5.5x restriction on payouts. The parent cash-flow report reads that restriction as blocking management fees and distributions from the Rhode Island entities to the parent when the ratio is breached, which would turn the best asset into a cash trap for the parent. If that reading is right, Bally's parent cannot use its most reliable cash flow to service debt while leverage exceeds 5.5x, and the moat protects GLPI's rent (2.13x covered on Master Lease II) rather than parent liquidity. The report's own confidence is low and the regulator can lift the cap. I flag it, do not resolve it, and ask for research on it.

5. What the congruence means for the forecasts. Because both sets and the evidence line up, the forecast's weak points are known and few: Chicago's date and ramp, the refinancing terms, and the UK response to the generosity cuts. Nothing in the earlier sets points in a direction the evidence has since reversed. The memo can therefore be written from one model, with the capital-structure branch as its first switch, without hedging between competing readings of the business.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the sell-side and investors already articulate the case, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or how much of the case is known.

I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

F3015th of 15

Layer 4 · Stock market narrative

Judgement: The narrative knows the headline (a going-concern company with a covenant cliff, a flagship under construction and a controlling sponsor) and does not know the arithmetic. The sell-side articulates the individual dynamics in isolation and nobody integrates them: the AlphaSense search of broker research, filings and expert calls from October 2022 to November 2026 found "no unified analysis that integrates" the fourteen dynamics we asked about, and rated its confidence that they are articulated "low". The credit desks (Deutsche Bank above all) and the landlord's analysts (Deutsche Bank and Jefferies on GLPI) carry more of the analysis than the equity desks, and they frame it from the creditor's side ("an operator-level issue rather than asset impairment"; "the real estate is insulated"). Investor commentary is close to absent: the report found no Twitter or retail narrative worth citing, which fits a controlled company with a third of its shares public and about $1.3m a day traded. The decisive question, whether investors treat the equity as a claim on the operating business or as an option on Standard General, is answered by the price rather than by any commentator: a market capitalisation below the market value of the Bally's Intralot stake, a price that sits between the sponsor's 2024 bid and the capitulation low, a 60% rally on a 13% bridge loan, and a share price that moves with financing headlines and not with its sector (D-07) all say "option". For our investment case the gap is unfavourable in the primary window and ambiguous beyond it: the dynamics the narrative has not absorbed are mostly negative for the parent's equity (rent arriving before Chicago's earnings; the parent's own cash gap; the covenant mechanics; the sponsor's limited capital), so the narrative has further to deteriorate when they surface; the dynamics it has also missed in the other direction (about $85m a year of reported interest that is not cash; the revolver's 25% utilisation carve-out and equity cures; property-level coverage above peers) support the credit and a survival narrative more than they support the common equity.

The refinancing terms likely by 31 March 2027 Deutsche Bank (collateral enhancement, cash paydowns, a reduced revolver, higher spreads expected, May 2025 and since); no rating-agency action since August 2026; no broker names a price None Known as a cliff; unknown as terms. The 25% revolver carve-out, the two equity cures and the PIK option are in the 8-K and discussed by nobody Negative on price (13% new money); possibly positive on mechanics (a paydown below 25% utilisation sidesteps the leverage test; equity cures exist)
Standard General's intentions and minority protections Jefferies (March 2024: "limited addressable market for fundamental investors"; June 2024: governance concerns with the sponsor's CIO as chair); Deutsche Bank (March 2024: "multiple avenues" including holdco debt and equity infusions; July 2024: 47% rolled over) The rollover itself (47% of the float stayed in) is the only investor "commentary" Known as a theme; the sponsor's capital ($647m of discretionary assets) and its record of third-party, dilutive structures are not discussed Negative: a rescue will be on a third party's terms; positive only if a bid materialises
GLPI's public warning (29 October 2026) Deutsche Bank and Jefferies on GLPI (coverage 1.98x and 2.13x; "multiple third parties interested" in Chicago; "operator-level"); Benzinga's call transcript; The Real Deal ("GLPI unfazed") None on Bally's Well known to GLPI's investors, framed as reassurance for the landlord Negative for Bally's equity: the landlord's comfort is that it can replace the tenant
The Bronx funding Deutsche Bank (February 2024 and 2026: a construction loan with a REIT right of first refusal is the likely structure; GLPI will not fund the majority; family-office talks, September 2026); the WhiteHawk 8-K; local press on the parkland fight Local political commentary Known as a headline ($4bn; a $500m fee paid); the 18-month WhiteHawk maturity, the 5% bond, the September 2027 construction deadline and the unnamed equity partner are not discussed Negative: a second maturity wall in April 2028 and a construction deadline that forces spending
Dilution of Bally's stake by Evoke's share consideration Deutsche Bank (June 2026: pro forma leverage 4.5x; the stake falls as shares are issued); the scheme document None Known to the subsidiary's analysts; not reflected in Bally's coverage Mildly negative (58% to about 51%)
UK player response to the generosity cuts and leakage Deutsche Bank (January 2026: management "may have underestimated" the feedback loop; Regulus's 12% decline forecast for 2027, August 2026); Jefferies and Bernstein (the Netherlands precedent; the 30% tax threshold); Morgan Stanley (June 2026: strong April and May) Expert calls (VIP leakage to black-market sites) Well known as a sector theme; Bally's Intralot's own exposure (bingo-led, recreational, 11% marketing) is discussed by Deutsche Bank and Jefferies Two-sided; the Q3 peer prints lean positive
North America Interactive's durability Expert calls (Hard Rock Digital, PENN, FanDuel alumni: "significantly behind", acquisition costs up about 40%); Jefferies on Rush Street as the disciplined comparator None Known as "small and behind"; the 50% profit share to Bally's Intralot after breakeven is barely mentioned Mildly negative on the profit share; immaterial in size
Ohio and Illinois lottery transitions Deutsche Bank (January 2026: Ohio about $20m of EBITDA from mid-2027; Illinois €44m of revenue to October 2027); Jefferies (December 2025); the Plain Dealer (November 2024) None Known to the subsidiary's analysts Negative if Illinois is lost; already in our base for Ohio

2. What the market prices, read from the price rather than the commentary. Four observations settle the "claim or option" question. First, the market capitalisation ($852m on 63.1m diluted shares) is below the market value of the 58% stake in Bally's Intralot (about $1.1bn to $1.4bn at €1.12), so the market assigns the parent's casinos, net of parent debt, a negative value and the equity's positive value to something else. Second, the shares fell 26% on the going-concern disclosure and rose about 60% from the $8.44 low on the WhiteHawk financing and GLPI's continued funding; a 13% pre-construction loan that worsens the fixed-charge arithmetic was received as good news, which means the price responds to liquidity, not to solvency. Third, the price's co-movement with the sector is 1% explained and its correlation even with the subsidiary is 0.23 (D-07). Fourth, the price has settled between Standard General's $15 proposal (March 2024) and the $18.25 it paid (February 2025), with 47% of the float having chosen to roll into the sponsor's vehicle. The equity is held and priced as an option on the sponsor and on Chicago. That also explains the unsecured bonds at 63% to 70% of par while the equity trades at $13.52: the credit market prices a haircut on the unsecured notes and the equity market prices the sponsor's incentive to keep control, which are not inconsistent; in Station Casinos the sponsors kept management while public equity was extinguished, and the bond and equity markets were both right.

3. Why the sell-side narrative is incomplete, and why that is unlikely to change inside 18 months. Equity coverage is five brokers, three of them stale, all Hold or Sell; the two houses with the deepest history (Deutsche Bank and Jefferies) now cover Bally's only through the credit desk and through GLPI and Bally's Intralot. The incentive to build a parent-level model is weak (a controlled company, a thin float, no banking fees from a sponsor that uses private credit). The integrated analysis therefore exists only in GLPI's and Bally's Intralot's own filings and in this work. The dynamics most likely to be articulated next are the ones with dated public triggers: the Q3 liquidity figure, the 27 November rebase question, the 10 December Chicago deadline, and the March 2027 refinancing. Each will be reported as an event, not as arithmetic, so the narrative will remain "distressed, controlled, complex" rather than "structurally short of fixed-charge coverage even after Chicago", which is the narrative our forecasts imply.

4. Secondarily, 18 months to three years out. The longer-dated dynamics (Chicago's stabilised economics after the terminals, the Bronx capital stack, the 2029 notes' springing maturity on the term loan, Evoke's 2028 refinancing, the Illinois contract) are discussed by Deutsche Bank's credit desk and by the subsidiary's analysts, and by nobody on the equity side. The one longer-dated dynamic the equity narrative does carry is the Chicago upside ($250m of EBITDAR; "transformative"), and it carries it at management's number rather than at Deutsche Bank's own post-hotel-pause caution. Beyond 18 months the gap between our assessment and the narrative is therefore larger on the upside the narrative believes in (Chicago at $250m against our $150m) than on the downside it has not absorbed, which is an unfavourable configuration for a long case and a favourable one only for a case built on the credit or on the landlord.

5. For the memo's topics #7, #8 and #10. Scope for narrative upgrades over 6 to 12 months exists only through a capital-structure event (a bridge announced as "liquidity ensured" has already been shown to lift the shares; a sponsor bid would do more); scope for narrative downgrades exists through every dated operating event. Momentum in the narrative is negative in the sector (Flutter and Entain's de-rating, prediction markets, UK duty) and separately negative at the company (the going-concern note, the CFO exit, GLPI's warning), with the company's own price having recovered on a financing headline rather than on any change in the facts. The lifecycle reading is "bottomed on a bridge, not turning up on fundamentals", and the narrative is not yet articulating why the bridge does not solve the problem.

Layer 1: Assessing the foundational components of an investment case

Written 3 November 2026, 10:51 to 11:10 UK, Opus, ricardinho login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to investigate and research each question, analyse the relevant information and form an insightful judgement regarding it, and to do the same for each group of questions as a whole, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating model and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Answers to the first set of questions: Bally's Corporation (BALY-US), as at 3 November 2026

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Notes for the Consumer, to read before the answers

1. The period is confirmed. The latest Earnings Review (files 05) covers the quarter ended 30 June 2026, reported on 14 August 2026 after a late-filing notice on 11 August 2026. That matches the FactSet block's last period (06/2026). The earlier review (files 06) covers Q1 2026, reported on 18 May 2026. Q3 2026 results are not in the files. Bally's reported Q3 2025 on 10 November 2025, so Q3 2026 is probably imminent, yet the FactSet block shows the next results date as 25 February 2027. Check which is right before modelling the second half.

2. Bally's is two credits in one set of accounts, so build the model in two layers. The consolidated accounts include 100% of Bally's Intralot S.A. (Athens-listed, BYLOT-GR), of which Bally's owns about 58%. Bally's Intralot has its own funded debt (€1,761m at June 2026), its own covenants, its own leverage target (2.5x), its own rating (Fitch B+, senior secured on Rating Watch Negative), and it is ring-fenced from the parent. Cash reaches the parent only through dividends (58% of a €30m dividend paid in July 2026) and intercompany charges (Bally's Intralot owed Bally's €30.31m at June 2026, up from €11.74m at December 2025). Consolidated EBITDA therefore overstates what is available to service the parent's debt and rent. My split of H1 2026, calculated from the segment tables in files 05 and 06:

H1 2026, USD millions Segment EBITDAR Comment
Casinos & Resorts (US) 205.8 (Q1 96.2, Q2 109.6) carries all of the GLPI rent
North America Interactive -4.1 (Q1 -7.1, Q2 +3.0) 50% of profits go to Bally's Intralot B2C once breakeven is reached
Corporate & Other -24.1
Parent subtotal ("RemainCo") 177.6 about $355m annualised
Bally's Intralot B2B 37.0 ring-fenced, 58% owned
Bally's Intralot B2C 151.8 (Q1 87.1, Q2 64.7) ring-fenced, 58% owned; Q2 is the first UK duty quarter
Consolidated segment EBITDAR 366.4
Triple-net rent (GLPI) -119.1 (Q2 63.5, annualising about $254m) all of it at the parent
Adjusted EBITDA 247.3
Net interest -228.9 (Q2 119.0, annualising about $476m) of which Bally's Intralot net finance €69.6m (about $80m)

On this split (calculated; an inference, because Bally's does not publish parent-only accounts), the parent earns about $355m of EBITDAR a year, pays about $254m of rent and roughly $300m of interest (consolidated interest less Bally's Intralot's), and so runs a structural cash deficit of the order of $200m a year before maintenance capex, Chicago, the Bronx and any cash tax. Consolidated, H1 2026 segment EBITDAR of $366m covered rent plus interest of $348m only 1.05 times. This one fact explains the going-concern warning and governs most of the answers below.

3. Some source figures are unreliable. The latest Earnings Review (files 05) carries figures that cannot be traced and that conflict with the FactSet block: a "$2.00 to $2.50 EPS consensus" before Q2 (FactSet consensus has been negative throughout; FY2026 is -$8.11), a "management target of $4 to $5 normalised EPS for 2027-2028", "11.6x" described as actual leverage (it is a Deutsche Bank pro forma estimate of November 2025), a "$150 million to $160 million" annual UK headwind, and a "frozen" Chicago timeline (only the hotel tower and non-gaming elements are paused; the casino is still targeted for spring 2027). Treat all of those as unreliable. The Change Forces digest's "pending take-private" is stale: Standard General's merger completed on 7 February 2025 and Bally's stayed listed. Its Bloomberg targets from Deutsche Bank ($18) and Jefferies ($13) are also stale, because both firms dropped equity coverage (October 2024 and May 2025). FactSet (file 19) is the authority on consensus.

4. The share count needs establishing. FactSet shows 48.5m shares at end-2025 (48.4m after the February 2025 merger). The Q1 and Q2 2026 losses per share ($2.69 and $2.41) against net losses of $162m and $146m imply about 60m weighted shares, and FactSet's capital-structure snapshot uses 63.1m diluted shares (market capitalisation $852m at $13.50). The source of the roughly 12m extra shares is not in the files. Establish it before modelling per-share figures.

5. Leverage figures sit on different bases. Rent-adjusted net debt to EBITDAR: 7.3x (Deutsche Bank, late 2024), 7.7x (Jefferies, November 2024), 8.2x (March 2025), 11.6x pro forma (Deutsche Bank, November 2025), 14.0x pro forma for the new leases (Deutsche Bank). FactSet net debt to EBITDA: 18.1x at Q2 2026, with leases in debt and FactSet operating EBITDA. The direction is clear; the level depends on the basis. Where I convert currencies I use about 1.17 US dollars per euro and 1.33 per pound (my assumptions).

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 2

Research requests for the next set

Company: Bally's Corporation (BALY-US, NYSE: BALY): a US regional casino operator (Casinos & Resorts segment: 20 casinos in 11 states, including both Rhode Island casinos and a temporary Chicago casino, with a permanent Chicago casino due in 2027), controlled by Standard General, which consolidates about 58% of Athens-listed Bally's Intralot S.A. (Bally's Intralot B2C: UK-centred online casino, bingo and betting; Bally's Intralot B2B: lottery technology and services) and runs North America Interactive (US online casino and sports betting), with developments in the Bronx (New York) and on the former Tropicana Las Vegas site

Request: T-P1

Peers: PENN Entertainment (regional operator and GLPI tenant; relocated Hollywood Joliet in August 2025 and Hollywood Aurora in June 2026 in Chicagoland); Boyd Gaming (regional operator that owns most of its real estate; sold Sam's Town Shreveport to Bally's in August 2026); Caesars Entertainment (regional estate leased from VICI and GLPI; its operating company's 2015 Chapter 11 is a precedent for rent-burdened operators); Red Rock Resorts (Durango new-build ramp and expansion); Century Casinos (levered regional operator with a VICI master lease, selling assets to repay debt); Golden Entertainment (regional operator that sold real estate to VICI); Rush Street Interactive and PENN's theScore Bet (sub-scale US online operators, for interactive cost structure); Rush Street Gaming's Rivers Casino Des Plaines (private; the largest Chicago-area casino and the closest competitor to Bally's Chicago); Station Casinos (2009-2011 restructuring of a levered developer-operator); Bally's Intralot S.A. and Evoke plc (the consolidated subsidiary and its pending acquisition, for UK online cost structure under the 40% duty) Inputs: What share of a US regional casino portfolio's operating costs is fixed (labour under collective bargaining, property overheads, triple-net rent with escalators, minimum technology and sponsorship commitments) and what share is variable (gaming taxes, promotional reinvestment, marketing)? How did property EBITDAR, and EBITDA after rent, move with gaming revenue at regional operators in 2008-2010, 2020 and 2022-2026, and what were the decremental margins? For newly opened or relocated casinos on triple-net leases, how did revenue, promotional reinvestment, EBITDAR, rent and EBITDA after rent evolve over the first 12, 24 and 36 months: PENN Hollywood Aurora (2026) and Joliet (2025), Rivers Des Plaines (2011), Hard Rock Rockford, MGM National Harbor, Encore Boston Harbor, Resorts World Catskills, Revel Atlantic City, and Bally's own temporary Chicago casino at Medinah Temple (2023-2026)? What did Bally's cost programmes promise and deliver: the 2023 interactive restructuring (up to 15% of the interactive workforce; $20.7m of charges in the first nine months of 2023), "Bally's 2.0" (2025), the corporate savings claimed after the Intralot transaction, and North America Interactive's move to the White Hat and Kambi platforms? How much of Bally's Corporate & Other costs could be removed? Have tenants of GLPI or VICI achieved rent reductions or deferrals outside a restructuring (Caesars-VICI lease amendments 2017-2022, PENN-GLPI amendments, Century-VICI)? What happens to regional casino share and margins when maintenance capex is deferred under high leverage? Which cost lines are cuttable in UK online after the April 2026 Remote Gaming Duty rise (generosity, marketing, content fees, platform costs), as in Bally's Intralot's mitigation plan disclosed with its H1 2026 results? In precedents where levered casino operators restructured (Station Casinos 2009-2011, Caesars Entertainment Operating Co 2015-2017, Tropicana Entertainment 2008-2010, Revel 2014), how much rent and interest was removed, and over what period? Focus: How much of Bally's US casino, interactive and corporate cost base, including rent and interest, can realistically be reduced over the next 18 months to four years without losing revenue; and, from dated precedents, how quickly a new triple-net-leased urban casino like Bally's Chicago moves from a rent-burdened opening to a positive contribution after rent.

Request: T-P2

Peers: PENN Entertainment, Boyd Gaming, Caesars Entertainment and Red Rock Resorts (regional casino pricing, hold and promotional reinvestment); Rush Street Gaming's Rivers Casino Des Plaines (private; Chicago-area competitor); Accel Entertainment (largest video gaming terminal operator in Illinois, with about 44% of Chicago location approvals per its press release); DraftKings, FanDuel (Flutter) and Rush Street Interactive (US online promotional intensity); Entain, Flutter, Evoke and Rank Group (UK online operators passing the 2026 duty rise through to players); Bally's Intralot S.A. (the subsidiary carrying Bally's UK online business) Operating conditions: The doubling of UK Remote Gaming Duty from 21% to 40% on 1 April 2026, passed through via lower bonuses and return-to-player, followed by the rise in remote General Betting Duty from 15% to 25% in April 2027; new or relocated competing supply in a local market (PENN's Aurora and Joliet relocations, Chicago's legalisation of video gaming terminals in 2026, new openings in Shreveport and Dover); a consumer squeeze on low- and middle-income drive-in customers from energy-led inflation and high rates (University of Michigan sentiment at 46.3 in October 2026); the opening and ramp-up of a new urban casino that uses promotional reinvestment to build its database; state and municipal tax increases on online sports betting (Illinois' per-wager tax from 1 January 2026; Chicago's municipal rate raised from 2% to 10.25% in 2026); a protected monopoly market (Bally's Rhode Island casinos and its exclusive online casino licence to 2043) Inputs: What evidence exists, dated and quantified, of Bally's raising or cutting slot hold, table minimums, free play, promotional reinvestment and non-gaming prices at its properties in 2023-2026, and how did that compare with PENN, Boyd and Caesars in the same markets? What were the revenue and margin effects of Bally's deliberate reduction of lower-tier and unrated play (described by Deutsche Bank in May 2025)? What promotional reinvestment rates have new urban casinos needed during their ramp-up, and how does Deutsche Bank's published estimate of slot promotional spend at 13.5% of gaming revenue for Bally's Chicago compare with precedents? How did the spread of video gaming terminals in Illinois since 2012 affect slot win and promotions at nearby casinos (Rivers, Hollywood Aurora and Joliet, Grand Victoria), and what does that imply for downtown Chicago? How did PENN's Aurora (June 2026) and Joliet (August 2025) relocations affect neighbouring casinos' revenue, share and promotions? In the UK after April 2026, what happened to active players, average revenue per player, deposits and market share for operators that cut bonuses and return-to-player, compared with the precedents of the 2014 Point of Consumption tax, the 2019 rise to 21% and the Netherlands' 2025 rise to 34.2%? Has Bally's Intralot's UK constant-currency growth (10.5% in Q1 2026, 11.6% in Q2 2026, per company releases) held through the H2 2026 generosity cuts? Do Rhode Island's regulatory agreements and revenue-sharing terms allow Bally's to change pricing there? How has Bally's responded on promotions to share losses in Missouri (August 2026), Atlantic City and Shreveport? How has prediction-market bidding for search keywords changed customer acquisition costs for smaller US online operators in 2026? Focus: Whether Bally's has any demonstrable pricing power in its US casinos and online businesses when facing new competing supply and a consumer squeeze, especially at its Chicago casino as video gaming terminals spread; and whether Bally's Intralot's UK bonus and return-to-player cuts are holding player volumes or losing them to rivals and unlicensed sites.

Request: T-P3

Why requested: Bally's outlook is shaped by self-reinforcing loops between leverage, rent, reinvestment and market share, and by how its controlling shareholder, its landlord and its lenders respond; a line-by-line forecast will miss these unless the loops and their precedents are understood. Peers: Station Casinos (2009-2011 restructuring of a levered developer-operator that kept its controlling owners); Caesars Entertainment Operating Co (2015 Chapter 11 and the disputes between parent and subsidiary creditors); Revel Atlantic City (urban new-build that failed under its capital structure); Century Casinos (levered regional operator with a master lease, divesting assets); PENN Entertainment (relocation-led share capture in Chicagoland); Gaming and Leisure Properties and VICI Properties (landlords' behaviour with stressed tenants); Bally's Intralot S.A. (the subsidiary's deleveraging and its exposure to the parent's cash needs); Evoke plc (a sub-scale UK operator that entered a leverage and tax spiral and is being acquired) Inputs: A negative loop: high leverage and rent lead to deferred property maintenance and technology spending, then share loss to refreshed competitors, then lower EBITDAR and a higher cost of capital. What is the dated evidence of this at Bally's in 2023-2026 (Deutsche Bank's comments on deferred maintenance, Missouri share loss in August 2026, Atlantic City, expert comments on floor technology), and how far did the same loop run at precedents? A landlord loop: GLPI's development funding for Chicago (up to $940m at an 8.5% yield, per GLPI filings) and the sale-leasebacks (Lincoln in February 2026 adding $56.0m of annual rent; Kansas City and Shreveport in December 2024 adding $32.2m) raise the break-even point and require further asset sales. How have GLPI and VICI behaved with stressed tenants, and what did GLPI's 29 October 2026 Q3 filing say about Bally's? A parent-subsidiary loop: a levered controlling parent's cash needs (dividends, intercompany charges, share purchases) could stall the subsidiary's deleveraging. What do Bally's Intralot's H1 2026 report (payables to Bally's Corporation of €30.31m at June 2026, up from €11.74m), its debt documents' ring-fencing and Fitch's June 2026 Rating Watch Negative show? A positive loop: a successful Chicago ramp-up funds refinancing at lower rates and reinvestment; which new urban casinos de-levered their owners, and how quickly? A UK consolidation loop: the duty forces sub-scale exits, player databases transfer to scaled operators and acquisition costs fall, against the balancing loop of offshore leakage. A controlling-shareholder loop: Standard General's bids ($38 in 2022, $15 in 2024, the $18.25 merger completed in February 2025) and Soohyung Kim's move to Executive Chair in January 2026; what did controlling owners do when listed casino companies entered distress (Station Casinos 2009-2011; Apollo and TPG at Caesars 2014-2017)? A Chicago political loop: video gaming terminal legalisation leads to lower projected casino revenue, a paused hotel and lower city tax receipts, then further political responses (property-tax relief, tax rates). Focus: Which loop dominates Bally's over the next 18 to 36 months, the leverage-underinvestment-share-loss spiral or the Chicago-ramp deleveraging loop; and how controlling shareholders, landlords and lenders have behaved in comparable distressed casino situations, with dated evidence of each loop operating now.

Request: T-P4

Why requested: The gap between Bally's adjusted EBITDA and its earnings per share is very large (rent, interest, depreciation and amortisation, minorities in Bally's Intralot, tax and an uncertain share count), so per-share outcomes depend on these inputs more than on operations. Peers: PENN Entertainment (EPS bridge for an operator with heavy GLPI rent); Caesars Entertainment (interest and lease burden after the Eldorado merger); Century Casinos (financing-obligation accounting for its VICI master lease); Bally's Intralot S.A. (the subsidiary's own interest, amortisation, tax and minorities); Entain plc and Lottomatica Group (large gaps between adjusted and reported earnings after acquisitions); Gaming and Leisure Properties (lease terms and rent commencement as disclosed by the landlord) Inputs: Interest expense by tranche and its likely repricing: the $1.1bn term loan due 2031 (February 2026); the $500m of 11% first-lien notes due October 2028; the unsecured notes outstanding; the revolving credit facility's size, drawn amount and maturity (Fitch referred to an October 2026 maturity) and whether it was extended; the WhiteHawk-led $560m Bronx financing ($400m term loan and $160m delayed draw at SOFR plus 8.50%, September-October 2026); interest-rate hedges; the terms and conditions of the net leverage covenant waiver to 31 March 2027 and the minimum-liquidity requirement; capitalised interest on Chicago and the Bronx. Focus: What a defensible reported and adjusted EPS bridge for FY2026 to FY2029 looks like for Bally's, after rent, interest by tranche (including refinancing of the covenant-waived facilities), depreciation and amortisation, minorities in Bally's Intralot, tax and the share count; and how it changes if Bally's Intralot completes the Evoke acquisition.

Layer 2: Translating the foundational components into an operating and financial model for the next 18 months, and 18 months to 3 years out

Written 3 November 2026, 20:14 to 20:34 UK, Opus, ricardinho login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years, based on the first set's answers, a base of the company's historical financial and operating performance, and how businesses in the sector and industry outperform or underperform, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a trustworthy judgement regarding the predictability of delivery of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Answers to the second set of questions: Bally's Corporation (BALY-US), as at 3 November 2026

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Notes for the Consumer, to read before the answers

1. The basis of every forecast in these answers. All money is in US dollars, consolidated, US GAAP, and excludes the pending Evoke acquisition (an overlay is given in P-09 and P-10). "EBITDA" means the company's Adjusted EBITDA, which is after the triple-net rent paid to GLPI. That is the brokers' basis (FY2025 actual $504m, 19.0% margin; FY2026 consensus $484m). "EBITDAR" is before that rent. Under US GAAP the GLPI leases are operating leases, so rent sits inside operating costs and is not a below-EBITDA lease payment. My EPS is "adjusted": it includes all depreciation and amortisation (the brokers' FY2026 consensus only reconciles if their EPS also includes acquired-intangible amortisation: EBITDA $484m less D&A $379m gives their EBIT of $105m to $115m). It excludes gains on sale-leasebacks, foreign-exchange gains and losses, fair-value moves on the Star stake, debt-extinguishment losses and the transaction, development, restructuring and Tropicana closure costs that the company adds back. It is after minorities.

2. Three facts the first set left open, now settled from the files, with one disagreement.

  • The share count: weighted basic and diluted shares were 60.59m in Q2 2026, against 48.99m shares in issue. The difference is 11.62m Sinclair "penny warrants", exercisable for almost nothing and therefore counted in basic shares (AlphaSense EPS report, citing the Q2 2026 10-Q). FactSet's 48.5m excludes them, and its 63.1m diluted count adds equity awards. Per-share figures in these answers use about 60.6m, rising about 0.5m a year from equity awards.
  • The revolver: commitments of $519.3m in early 2026, $303.75m drawn at 30 June 2026, one tranche maturing in October 2026 and the other in October 2028 (Q1 and Q2 2026 10-Qs). The waiver requires minimum liquidity of $325m to 30 September 2026 and $300m for the quarter to 31 December 2026 (8-K, 5 August 2026). Whether the October 2026 tranche was extended is still not in the files.
  • The 11% first-lien notes: I disagree with the first set. The first set lists $500m of 11% notes due October 2028 as outstanding. The AlphaSense EPS report, citing the 10-Q, says they were redeemed in full in October 2025 with Intralot transaction proceeds. The Q2 2026 debt table it describes holds only the $1.1bn term loan due 2031, the $750m 5.625% notes due 2029 (carrying value $599.0m), the $750m 5.875% notes due 2031 (carrying value $530.8m) and the revolver. I treat the 11% notes as repaid. This does not move my interest forecast, because I anchor interest on the actual Q2 2026 charge ($119.0m), not on a reconstruction of the debt stack. The Consumer should still verify it in the Q3 2026 10-Q.

3. A non-cash item that matters for the cash flow bridge. The 2029 and 2031 notes carry about $370m of discount from the fair-value step-down when Standard General's merger completed (face $1.5bn, carrying $1.13bn). It accretes through interest expense at about $85m a year. Reported interest therefore overstates cash interest by about $85m a year until 2029. Parent cash interest is about $230m a year, not the roughly $316m that the parent's share of reported interest suggests (my inference from the carrying values and the Q2 run-rate).

4. Q3 2026 results are imminent. The first set flagged FactSet's next results date of 25 February 2027 as suspect. Bally's reported Q3 2025 on 10 November 2025, so Q3 2026 should arrive within days. That is the first test of everything below (P-06).

5. The capital structure is the first branch of the model. My base case (P-10) is a debt-funded bridge: covenant reset by 31 March 2027, plus about $450m of new secured private credit at about 13%. I give it 45% probability over 18 months. The alternatives are a Standard General-backed equity recapitalisation (20%), a restructuring (20%), a take-private (10%) and a clean refinancing below a 10% blended cost (5%). The operating forecasts are the same in every branch; interest, share count and equity value are not.

Notes to Part 1 (read before using the inputs):

  • EBITDA margin is the company's Adjusted EBITDA, which is after the triple-net rent paid to GLPI. This is the brokers' basis: FY2025 actual $504m, 19.0%; FY2026 consensus $484m, 15.3%. The underlying EBITDA after rent is $483m, $459m, $486m, $548m and $588m. Rent inside it is -$248m, -$302m, -$346m, -$351m and -$357m.
  • Depreciation and amortisation is the total, including acquired-intangible amortisation. This departs from the form's convention, for two reasons. First, the brokers' EPS for Bally's includes it: consensus EBITDA $484m less D&A $379m reconciles to consensus EBIT of $105m to $115m. Second, the split is not disclosed reliably. On the form's convention, the excluded amortisation would be roughly $150m to $250m a year (the Intralot and Gamesys intangibles), and EPS would be about $2.5 to $4 a share better than consensus's basis.
  • Net financial items are reported net interest. They include about $85m a year (FY2026 to FY2028), falling to about $65m (FY2029) and $45m (FY2030), of non-cash accretion of the fair-value discount on the 2029 and 2031 notes. They exclude FX, Star fair-value moves and debt-extinguishment items. They assume a debt-funded bridge: a covenant reset by 31 March 2027 plus about $450m of new secured private credit at about 13%, with further debt-funded deficits of about $330m a year at about 13% from FY2028.
  • Tax rate is the tax charge over pre-tax profit, on FactSet's sign convention. A negative rate means a tax charge despite a pre-tax loss: $25m, $35m, $40m, $45m and $50m. This is cash tax in the UK, Gibraltar, Greece and Turkey, with no shield from US losses (full valuation allowance).
  • Share count: +12.1m in FY2026 takes FactSet's FY2025 count of 48.5m to the EPS denominator of about 60.6m. That is 11.62m penny warrants, counted in basic shares because they are exercisable for almost nothing, plus about 0.5m of equity awards. After that, +0.5m a year from awards. If the model starts from a different opening count, the target weighted shares are 60.6m, 61.1m, 61.6m, 62.1m and 62.6m.
  • The model has no minorities line. Bally's Intralot's minorities (about 42%) currently absorb losses: +$20m (FY2026), +$20m (FY2027), +$12m, +$5m, then 0. The model's EPS will therefore be about $0.3 a share more negative than my cross-check in FY2026 and FY2027.
  • Perimeter: excludes the pending Evoke acquisition (overlay in Part 4).

Nothing from FY2027: asset sales (Star stake, Tropicana rights, a Bally's Intralot sell-down) are possible but not in the base.
  • Buybacks less issuance: FY2026 is the H1 actual (-$24.4m, mainly shares withheld for tax). No equity raise is in the base. The main alternative (20% probability) is a Standard General-backed raise of about $500m at about $8 a share from mid-2027, which would add about 62m shares and cut net financial items by about $40m to $60m a year.

The operating drivers behind the inputs

Casinos & Resorts:

  • Core estate revenue is flat in FY2027 and grows 0.5% to 1% a year after. US regional gaming revenue was +2.5% in September 2026, and Morgan Stanley expects it flat in 2027. Bally's lost Missouri share (August 2026) and Atlantic City keeps shrinking. Maintenance capex is below the industry norm.
  • The core margin falls 30bp in FY2027 and 20bp in FY2028 (labour, defensive reinvestment, no pricing power).

Chicago permanent casino:

  • Opens at the start of Q3 2027, one quarter after the "spring 2027" guidance, given the delivery record.
  • Year one: 65% of stabilised revenue at a 20% EBITDAR margin.
  • Stabilised: $500m of revenue and $150m of EBITDAR, about 60% of Deutsche Bank's $250m. The haircut reflects the Chicago video gaming terminals, PENN's Aurora and Joliet relocations, the hotel cut to about 100 rooms, and a temporary casino running at about a quarter of its EBITDAR target.
  • New rent of $80m a year (8.5% on $940m) from opening. The $20m land rent and the $14.6m Tropicana rent are already in the base.
  • Net effect on EBITDA after rent: -$28m in year one, turning positive in year two.

North America Interactive:

  • Revenue +12% in FY2027, then +10%, +9% and +8%, led by iGaming.
  • The margin builds to 7.5% on variable-cost platforms.
  • Prediction-market keyword bidding raises acquisition costs.

Bally's Intralot B2C:

  • FY2026 H2 revenue includes Bilyoner (Turkey) until it laps in Q4 2026.
  • UK growth slows from +11.6% (constant currency, Q2 2026) to mid-single digits after the H2 2026 generosity cuts.
  • The General Betting Duty rises to 25% in April 2027.
  • Sterling is held flat. A 10% move changes B2C EBITDAR by about $29m.
  • The margin recovers from 26.6% (Q2 2026) to about 29% to 30% as the €50m mitigation plan and the €41m merger synergies land.

Bally's Intralot B2B:

  • Ohio exits in mid-2027 (about €20m a year of EBITDA).
  • Re-tenders reprice down; Victoria starts in 2027.
  • The Illinois expiry (October 2027) is a risk not in the base.

Rent: Lincoln (+$56m a year, from February 2026) is in the FY2026 base. Escalators are about 1.5% a year (CPI-linked, 1% floor, 2% cap).

Interest:

  • The anchor is the Q2 2026 actual ($119.0m a quarter). Of that, Bally's Intralot is about $40m a quarter.
  • The WhiteHawk Bronx loan ($400m at SOFR plus 850, from October 2026; $160m delayed draw) is half expensed in FY2027 and capitalised from FY2028.
  • A covenant reset adds about 250bp on about $1.4bn.
  • New money of about $450m at about 13% comes in Q2 2027, with further debt-funded deficits of about $330m a year from FY2028.
  • The 5.625% 2029 notes are refinanced at about 12%.

Capital-structure branches over 18 months (probabilities):

  • The debt-funded bridge, used in these inputs: 45%.
  • A Standard General-backed equity recapitalisation: 20%.
  • A restructuring: 20%.
  • A take-private: 10%.
  • A clean refinancing below a 10% blended cost: 5%.

The company's guidance, and how your inputs compare with it

Bally's itself gives no financial guidance for FY2026 or FY2027. Its last formal guidance was for FY2024, and it was cut repeatedly in 2022 and 2023. The informal milestones and how my inputs treat them:

  • Chicago permanent casino in "spring 2027": I assume the start of Q3 2027.
  • North America Interactive on a "route to profitability": this is in my inputs, reaching a 7.5% margin by FY2030.
  • "Steady foundational revenue" from the temporary casino: it earns about $13m of EBITDAR a year against a $50m target.
  • The Q2 2026 10-Q states that Bally's does not project meeting its liquidity-maintenance requirements over the next twelve months without new financing. My base assumes that financing is debt at about 13%.

Bally's Intralot, consolidated:

  • Guides FY2026 adjusted EBITDA of about €422m (IFRS, its own definition, reaffirmed May 2026) and a FY2027 pro forma target of €454m, excluding Evoke.
  • My B2C plus B2B segment EBITDAR on Bally's US GAAP reporting is $369m in FY2026 (about €315m) and $362m in FY2027. That is not directly comparable: IFRS 16 puts leases below EBITDA, the capitalised development policy differs, and Bally's allocates some costs differently.
  • On the same direction of travel, my inputs imply that the subsidiary's guidance is at risk of a rebase, either at its Q3 results on 27 November 2026 or with FY2026 results. Its own Interactive outlook (€280m to €295m) plus a lottery run-rate of about €104m sums to €384m to €399m.

Against consensus (FactSet median, three to five brokers):

FY2026 FY2027 FY2028
Revenue 1% below 4% below 5% below
EBITDA after rent in line 15% below 23% below
Net interest (consensus about $460m implied) about $120m higher about $140m higher

FY2026 EPS is better on my adjusted basis only because consensus includes some H1 one-offs.

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

1. EBITDA.

  • The company's "Adjusted EBITDA" is segment Adjusted EBITDAR less triple-net rent. It excludes transaction costs, development and pre-opening costs, restructuring, Tropicana closure costs, share-based pay, gains on sale-leasebacks, FX and other non-operating items.
  • This is the brokers' basis: FY2025 $504m on both.
  • FactSet's history line (operating EBITDA, FY2025 $344m) is 46.5% lower, because it does not add back those items. Never compute growth from FactSet's history to consensus; use the brokers'-basis actuals.
  • Q1 2026: Adjusted EBITDAR $178.9m, less rent $55.6m, gives Adjusted EBITDA $123.3m. Q2 2026: $187.5m less $63.5m gives $124.0m.

2. EBIT and pre-tax.

  • Consensus EBIT ($115m in FY2026) equals consensus EBITDA less total D&A. The brokers do not add back acquired-intangible amortisation for Bally's, so my D&A input is total D&A.
  • Consensus pre-tax (-$515m in FY2026) includes at least part of H1 2026's non-operating items: the Q1 non-operating charge of -$169.3m, the +$105.8m Lincoln gain and the +$21.1m FX gain.
  • My pre-tax (-$376m in FY2026) excludes them, so FY2026 is not like for like. FY2027 onward is closer to like for like.

3. EPS. Four bases exist:

  • reported basic (FY2025 -$14.45; H1 2026 -$5.10);
  • FactSet adjusted diluted (FY2025 -$10.60);
  • brokers' adjusted (FY2025 -$11.59; FY2026 consensus -$8.11, with brokers ranging from -$6.94 to -$9.36);
  • mine, which is adjusted, after minorities, on about 60.6m shares including the 11.62m penny warrants.

My FY2026 reported EPS estimate is about -$9 to -$10. My adjusted figure is -$6.28.

4. Interest. Reported net interest includes about $85m a year of non-cash accretion on the 2029 and 2031 notes. Free cash flow on my definition uses cash interest; the model will use the reported figure, so its free cash flow will be about $85m a year lower than mine through FY2028 for that reason alone.

5. Bally's Intralot. It reports under IFRS in euros: €1,761m of debt at June 2026, 4.05x pro forma net leverage, guidance of €422m. Bally's consolidates it under US GAAP in dollars, with negative IFRS-to-GAAP adjustments of about $7m. Use Bally's segment figures for this model.

6. Evoke overlay (not in the inputs). If it completes around 1 April 2027:

USD m FY2027 FY2028 FY2029
Revenue about +1,750 about +2,350 about +2,400
EBITDA after rent about +250 about +420 about +470
D&A about -130 about -180 about -180
Interest about -170 about -230 about -230

Bally's stake falls to about 51% in an all-share outcome, and the EPS effect is about $0 net.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Bally's Corporation (BALY-US, NYSE: BALY): a US regional casino operator controlled by Standard General. Its Casinos & Resorts segment has 20 casinos in 11 states, including both Rhode Island casinos, a temporary Chicago casino and a permanent Chicago casino under construction, mostly on GLPI triple-net master leases. North America Interactive is its US online casino and sports betting business. It consolidates about 58% of Athens-listed Bally's Intralot S.A.: Bally's Intralot B2C is UK-centred online casino, bingo and betting; Bally's Intralot B2B is lottery technology and services; the subsidiary is acquiring Evoke plc. Bally's also has developments in the Bronx (New York) and on the former Tropicana Las Vegas site, and a stake of about 38% in The Star Entertainment Group.

Request: T-D1

Peers: PENN Entertainment (GLPI tenant; relocated Hollywood Joliet in August 2025 and Hollywood Aurora in June 2026, the closest precedent for a funded Chicagoland ramp); Boyd Gaming (owns most of its real estate at low leverage; sold Sam's Town Shreveport to Bally's in August 2026); Caesars Entertainment (VICI and GLPI tenant; its operating company's 2015 to 2017 Chapter 11 is the main precedent for a rent- and debt-burdened operator); Red Rock Resorts (Durango new-build ramp); Century Casinos (levered VICI tenant selling assets to cut debt and rent); Rush Street Gaming's Rivers Casino Des Plaines (private; largest Chicago-area casino and Bally's Chicago's closest competitor); Hard Rock Casino Rockford (Illinois casino that moved from a temporary to a permanent building in 2026); MGM National Harbor and Encore Boston Harbor (urban new-build ramps); Station Casinos (2009 to 2011 restructuring of a levered developer-operator that kept its controlling owners); Bally's Intralot S.A., Evoke plc, Entain and Rank Group (UK online operators passing the April 2026 duty rise through to players) Operating conditions: a large new urban casino opening on a triple-net lease, into a metro market where video gaming terminals were legalised in 2026 and a rival has relocated two casinos; UK online after Remote Gaming Duty rose from 21% to 40% (1 April 2026) and before remote General Betting Duty rises from 15% to 25% (April 2027); a squeeze on low- and middle-income US drive-in consumers (University of Michigan sentiment 46.3, October 2026); refinancing in a high-yield market where private credit lends at SOFR plus 8.5%; a covenant waiver that expires on 31 March 2027 alongside a going-concern disclosure (Q2 2026 10-Q) Inputs:

  • How plausible is it that Bally's Chicago permanent casino (topped off July 2026, guided to open in spring 2027, hotel and about 12% of amenities paused) reaches the earnings that sell-side models assume? Weigh this against the record of Bally's temporary Medinah Temple casino against its own targets and the City of Chicago's revenue projections since September 2023.
  • How have newly opened or relocated urban and regional casinos performed in their first 12, 24 and 36 months against the estimates published before they opened? Cover Hollywood Aurora, Hollywood Joliet, Hard Rock Rockford, MGM National Harbor, Encore Boston Harbor, Rivers Des Plaines, Resorts World Catskills and Revel. How did promotional reinvestment behave during each ramp?
  • What does Bally's record of delivery show? Cover guidance cuts in 2022 and 2023, consensus EBITDAR misses in each quarter of 2024, North America Interactive's breakeven (promised from 2023, reached in Q2 2026), the timeline of the temporary and permanent Chicago casinos, and Bally's Intralot's FY2025 delivery against its €435m target.
  • Is North America Interactive's move to positive segment EBITDAR (+$3.0m in Q2 2026 on revenue up 16.9%) durable while the market leaders raise marketing spend and prediction-market operators bid up search keywords?
  • Has Bally's Intralot's UK business kept its growth (10.5% and 11.6% in constant currency in Q1 and Q2 2026, about 13% in July 2026, per company releases) after cutting player bonuses and return-to-player in H2 2026?
  • How many regional or urban casino operators with lease-adjusted leverage above about 7x, and a development project under way, returned to sustained positive earnings without a restructuring, an equity injection or a large asset sale? How long did it take, and what set the successes apart from the failures?
  • Does Bally's have the management capacity to deliver? Its chief financial officer resigned with effect from 4 September 2026, the President is interim CFO, the treasurer left in April 2026, and the CEO also runs Bally's Intralot. Focus: Whether Bally's can deliver a profitable Chicago ramp and a resilient UK business on the strengths it has demonstrably shown, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire. Also, whether any comparable leveraged casino operator has closed a gap between earnings and fixed charges (rent plus interest) of this kind without a capital-structure event.

Request: T-D2

Peers: PENN Entertainment (GLPI rent burden and relocation ramps); Caesars Entertainment (regional master lease near 1.0x rent coverage per Jefferies, 11 January 2026; 2015 restructuring); Century Casinos (master-lease operator at about 8x lease-adjusted leverage); Golden Entertainment (sale-leaseback to VICI in April 2026); Station Casinos, Tropicana Entertainment and Revel Atlantic City (distressed precedents and the dispersion of their outcomes); Gaming and Leisure Properties and VICI Properties (landlord behaviour with stressed tenants); Bally's Intralot S.A., Evoke plc and Entain (UK duty mitigation outcomes); Rivers Casino Des Plaines and Accel Entertainment (Chicago competition) Operating conditions: the first year of a new urban casino whose rent begins at opening; covenant waiver expiry and refinancing between Q4 2026 and Q1 2027; the UK generosity cuts in H2 2026 and the betting duty rise in April 2027; US lottery contract transitions (Ohio in mid-2027, Illinois in October 2027); sterling volatility; regional US gaming revenue slowing (+2.5% in September 2026 per Deutsche Bank) Inputs:

  • Rent by lease: the steps in Bally's rent and when they start. Lincoln added $56.0m a year from February 2026; Kansas City and Shreveport $32.2m from December 2024; the Chicago lease carries $20m of land rent plus an 8.5% yield on GLPI's construction funding of up to $940m ($640.7m funded at 30 September 2026). GLPI's Q3 2026 10-Q reports $70.5m of deferred rental income on that funding. When does Chicago rent begin in cash and in the income statement, and how is Bally's prepaid rent asset released?
  • Interest by tranche:
  • the $1.1bn term loan due 2031 and its swaps;
  • the $750m 5.625% notes due 2029 and the $750m 5.875% notes due 2031, carried below face after the 2025 merger, and the non-cash accretion through interest expense;
  • the revolver ($519.3m of commitments, $303.75m drawn at 30 June 2026, tranches maturing October 2026 and October 2028);
  • the WhiteHawk Bronx financing ($400m term loan and $160m delayed draw at SOFR plus 8.50%, 18-month maturity);
  • whether the $500m of 11% first-lien notes issued in February 2025 were redeemed in October 2025;
  • how much of this interest will be capitalised.
  • Dispersion of first-year outcomes: across new US casino openings since 2010, how widely did first-year EBITDAR differ from pre-opening estimates, and how often did a company forecast that rested on one opening meet its targets?
  • Dispersion after going-concern disclosures: for gaming and leisure companies that disclosed going-concern doubt or a covenant waiver, what was the spread of outcomes within 18 months (refinancing, equity recapitalisation, restructuring, take-private), and what decided which one occurred?
  • UK mitigation dispersion: how widely did UK operators' mitigation of the April 2026 duty rise differ (Entain 50%, Bally's Intralot 65%, Evoke, Rank), and how did revenue respond in the two quarters after generosity cuts?
  • Concentration: how concentrated are the drivers of Bally's FY2027 results? Cover Chicago's opening date and ramp, the refinancing terms, UK revenue, Ohio's exit, sterling and regional US gaming revenue, and what each implies for the width of the outcome range. Focus: How wide and how skewed the range of outcomes for Bally's FY2027 EBITDA after rent and EPS is, once the dated rent and interest step-ups are included; and which drivers decide where in that range the result falls.

Request: T-I1

Peers: Bally's Intralot S.A. (the only entity in the group that guides; results on 27 November 2026); PENN Entertainment, Caesars Entertainment, Boyd Gaming and Red Rock Resorts (Q3 2026 regional read-across); Entain, Flutter, Evoke plc and Rank Group (UK online after the duty rise); Lottomatica (acquirer integration and guidance precedent); Gaming and Leisure Properties (landlord disclosures on Bally's) Operating conditions: a company giving no guidance while under a going-concern disclosure; Q3 2026 results (Bally's reported Q3 2025 on 10 November 2025) and FY2026 results; the covenant waiver expiry on 31 March 2027; the Chicago opening in 2027; completion of the Evoke acquisition (shareholders approved, timing late 2026 or early 2027); the UK generosity cuts and the April 2027 betting duty step Inputs:

Focus: Whether Bally's Intralot's FY2026 guidance will be rebased, and whether consensus FY2027 EBITDA and EPS for Bally's (median $541m and -$5.41 per FactSet, 30 October 2026) will move after the Q3 2026 results and the refinancing. Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 4 November 2026, 13:51 to 14:12 UK, Fable, ricky login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Answers to the third and fourth sets of questions: Bally's Corporation (BALY-US), as at 4 November 2026

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Notes for the Consumer, to read before the answers

1. The basis. These answers build on the first set (foundations), the second set (the operating and financial model), the Financial Analyst's model summary (update 1), the second set's forecast inputs, and the AlphaSense reports commissioned for this work. Money is in US dollars unless stated; "EBITDA" is the company's Adjusted EBITDA, after GLPI rent, which is the brokers' basis; "EBITDAR" is before rent. Bally's Q3 2026 results are not in the files. Bally's reported Q3 2025 on 10 November 2025, so Q3 2026 should arrive within days, and it is the first test of everything below. FactSet's "next results date" of 25 February 2027 still conflicts with that and should be ignored.

2. Facts the AlphaSense reports settle, correct or add. Each changes a detail in the earlier sets without changing their direction.

  • The $500m 11% first-lien notes were redeemed in full in October 2025 ($540.4m paid including a premium; a $57.4m extinguishment loss). The second set was right; the first set's debt stack was stale.
  • The revolver's commitments stepped down from $519.3m to about $319m in October 2026 on a scheduled partial maturity; the remaining commitments run to October 2028. $303.75m was drawn at 30 June 2026, leaving about $196m available then and only about $15m of undrawn commitment after the step-down if the drawn amount is unchanged. This answers the first set's open question: the October 2026 tranche was not extended; it matured.
  • The term loan ($1.1bn, due February 2031) costs Term SOFR plus 7.50% with a 3.0% floor, allows up to 3.5 points of the interest to be paid in kind, springs to 1 March 2029 if the 2029 unsecured notes are then outstanding, and is secured on the 58% stake in Bally's Intralot. The August 2026 amendment (8-K, 5 August 2026) sets minimum liquidity, defined as unrestricted cash plus unutilised revolver commitments, at $325m for the quarters to 30 September 2026 and $300m for the quarter to 31 December 2026; the first-lien net leverage covenant returns at 4.50x (4.00x after "Project Marathon" completes) with the compliance certificate for the quarter to 31 March 2027, is tested only in quarters when revolver utilisation exceeds 25% of commitments, and may be cured by equity at most twice in any four quarters.
  • The unsecured notes' fair values at 30 June 2026 were 69.6% of par (5.625% 2029 notes) and 63.0% (5.875% 2031 notes), which is where the credit market prices a haircut. No rating-agency action on Bally's has been found since the August 2026 waiver; Fitch rates Bally's B- (from its June 2026 Evoke note) and Bally's Intralot B+.
  • The first set's reading that the parent was "drawing cash out of the subsidiary" (payables to Bally's rising from €11.74m to €30.31m) was wrong. The rise is the accrual of Bally's €17.8m share of the €30m dividend approved on 28 May 2026 and paid on 27 July 2026. The intercompany channel is ordinary.
  • Bally's Intralot's bond indenture limits restricted payments to a basket built from 50% of consolidated net income plus a €40m general basket; its revolver carries a 4.75x springing net leverage test; its US lottery entities are unrestricted under the indenture. Parent covenant breaches do not cross-default it.
  • The Rhode Island regulatory agreement caps parent gross debt at 5.50x consolidated Adjusted EBITDA; above that, dividends, buybacks and management fees to affiliates are blocked, with regulator discretion to lift the cap. The AlphaSense report rates its own confidence here "low"; I treat the reach of this restriction (whether it stops intra-group flows from the Rhode Island casinos) as unverified and important (see the research requests).
  • Standard General's discretionary assets under management were $647.32m at 31 December 2025 (Form ADV, 30 March 2026). That is smaller than Bally's market capitalisation plus the float it does not own, and a fraction of the $4.5bn of debt.
  • Bally's Chicago, Inc. is a separate SEC registrant (from the stalled minority IPO) and files its own 10-Q: the temporary casino earned $3.32m of Adjusted EBITDAR in Q2 2026 against $1.62m a year earlier, and $13.1m of adjusted gross receipts in July 2026. GLPI had funded $640.7m of its $940m by 30 September 2026 ($472.6m in the first nine months of 2026) and carried $70.5m of deferred Chicago rental income; about $400m of Bally's $1.34bn minimum spend remained at 30 June 2026 and management expects the total to exceed the minimum (about $1.7bn in press reports). The host community agreement's section 7.4 charges liquidated damages of 85% of the projected daily city tax less actual tax if 75% of the gaming floor is not open by the deadline (10 December 2026), with up to two three-month extensions and one two-month extension available. The temporary casino's four-year owner's licence expires on 25 October 2027. The Illinois Gaming Board had licensed 42 Chicago video-terminal locations by August 2026 (17 of them Accel's) and 78 by late October, against 311 applications; the city had issued no local permits, and terminals are expected live in late 2026 or early 2027. Deutsche Bank's pre-opening base case for the permanent casino was $160m of first-year EBITDAR with 500 hotel rooms (bear $100m, bull $195m); management's figure is $250m; the hotel is now cut to about 100 rooms for the opening.
  • The Bronx: the $500m licence and $115m golf concession were paid in Q1 2026; $161m of Ferry Point improvements are due within seven years; a bond or deposit of 5% of project cost (about $200m on $4bn) is required; physical construction must begin within 18 months of the March 2026 land transfer, that is by September 2027, or the bond and licence are at risk; Bally's signed a non-binding letter of intent with an unnamed equity partner in August 2026; the WhiteHawk facility ($400m drawn 2 October 2026 plus a $160m delayed draw, SOFR plus 8.50%) matures in 18 months, about April 2028.
  • Sector reference points: Caesars agreed to be acquired by Fertitta Entertainment at about 7.5x EBITDA (Deutsche Bank, 29 May 2026); UK Machine Games Duty goes to 25% from April 2027, far below the feared 40%; Entain's UK and Ireland online revenue grew 14% in Q3 2026 (iGaming +17%), Flutter's UK digital 14% in Q2, Rank's UK digital 12% in the quarter to September 2026, while Evoke's Q3 revenue fell 7% reported (2% like for like).

3. The two cross-check gaps the model summary flags, explained. Both are definitional and mechanical; neither shows a forecast input to be wrong, so I change no input and do not write inputs-changed.md.

  • FY2026 earnings per share: the Analyst's -6.28 against the model's -6.92. About $0.33 of the $0.64 gap is minorities: the model has no minorities line, and Bally's Intralot's 42% minority absorbs about $20m of FY2026 losses (the second set's figure without minorities is -6.61). The remaining $0.31 is the share count: the model's net income of -$408.1m implies a weighted count of about 59.0m shares, whereas the 11.62m penny warrants were already in the basic count from the start of 2026 (Q1 2026 reported EPS used about 60m), so the whole year should be on about 60.6m. The model appears to phase the +12.1m change through the year. In FY2027 to FY2030 the gap is within $0.05 of the minorities effect, so the mechanics are right from FY2027.
  • FY2026 free cash flow: the Analyst's -$1,025m against the model's -$867m. The model's figure reconciles exactly to net income (-$408m) plus depreciation and amortisation ($382m), less the working-capital build (about $20m) and capital spending (-$821m). The Analyst's figure additionally deducts about $250m of cash one-offs (the $98.9m of upfront licence fees, transaction and development costs, construction-period Chicago rent) and adds back about $85m of non-cash accretion on the 2029 and 2031 notes that sits inside reported interest. Net, -$165m against the -$158m gap. The same two items explain the later years (FY2027: -$100m of one-offs and +$85m of accretion; FY2028: -$40m and +$85m), which is why the model is less negative than the Analyst in FY2026 and more negative from FY2028. The model's free cash flow should therefore be read as "before one-offs, with accretion treated as cash"; the Analyst's is the better cash figure, and the model's FY2026 net-debt reconciliation difference of $365m is partly these items.

4. A phasing artefact in the model's quarters. The summary says FY2026's quarterly margins "were moved by +4.1 points to match the year". The cause is a basis mismatch: the model's H1 2026 actuals are FactSet's operating EBITDA ($98.6m and $85.1m, $183.7m in total), while the inputs and the annual figure are on the company's Adjusted EBITDA basis ($123.3m and $124.0m, $247.3m). To reach the year's $484.7m the model lifted H2 2026 by the $63.6m shortfall, so its Q3 and Q4 2026 margins read about 19.8% and 18.3% rather than my 15.7% and 14.2%. The annual figure is right; the quarterly profile is not. When Q3 2026 is reported (about $125m on the company's basis), it will look like a miss against the model's quarter but not against my input.

5. To the Financial Analyst. The Financial Analyst should (i) replace the model's H1 2026 EBITDA actuals with the company's Adjusted EBITDA ($123.3m for Q1 and $124.0m for Q2), which is the basis of the inputs and of consensus, so that the H2 2026 quarters are not force-fitted; (ii) apply the FY2026 share count of 60.6m to the whole year rather than phasing the +12.1m change, because the penny warrants were in the basic count from January 2026; and (iii) note in the memo that the model's free cash flow excludes cash one-offs and treats the roughly $85m a year of non-cash note accretion as cash, so that the Analyst's cross-check figures are used for the cash discussion.

6. The capital-structure branches, updated. The second set's base case is a debt-funded bridge (45%). After the AlphaSense evidence on Standard General's capacity, the revolver step-down, the covenant mechanics and the bond prices, my weights over 18 months are: debt-funded bridge, including an amend-and-extend with the private-credit lenders, 40%; a Standard General-led recapitalisation funded largely with third-party capital, on terms more dilutive than the second set's $8 placeholder, 20%; a balance-sheet restructuring (a distressed exchange of the unsecured notes, a debt-for-equity swap, or Chapter 11) 30%; a take-private 5%; a clean refinancing below a 10% blended cost 5%. The base-case operating inputs are the same in every branch, which is why I leave them unchanged.

Co-movement of BALY-US with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-03 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.
