Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Bally's Corporation (BALY-US, NYSE: BALY): a US regional casino operator controlled by Standard General, with a Casinos & Resorts segment of 20 casinos in 11 states (including both Rhode Island casinos, a temporary Chicago casino and a permanent Chicago casino under construction, mostly on GLPI triple-net master leases), a North America Interactive segment (US online casino and sports betting), and an approximately 58% consolidated stake in Athens-listed Bally's Intralot S.A. (Bally's Intralot B2C: UK-centred online casino, bingo and betting; Bally's Intralot B2B: lottery technology and services; acquiring Evoke plc), plus developments in the Bronx (New York) and on the former Tropicana Las Vegas site, and a stake of about 38% in The Star Entertainment Group.

Request: OWN-1

Question: What did Bally's Corporation's (BALY-US) third-quarter 2026 results, earnings call and Form 10-Q (expected in November 2026; Bally's reported its third quarter of 2025 on 10 November 2025) disclose, and how did the sell-side, the credit market and the landlord respond? Cover: liquidity at 30 September 2026 against the minimum-liquidity conditions of the August 2026 credit-agreement amendment (Bally's 8-K, 5 August 2026); the revolving credit facility's commitments and drawn balance after the scheduled October 2026 step-down (Bally's Q2 2026 10-Q); any change to the going-concern language, the covenant waiver that runs to the compliance certificate for the quarter ending 31 March 2027, or the refinancing plan; whether a further late-filing notice (Form NT 10-Q) was issued, as on 11 May and 11 August 2026; the stated opening date and remaining cost of the permanent Chicago casino, the status of the hotel tower paused in August 2026, and any update on the host community agreement's 10 December 2026 deadline and the city's video gaming terminals; segment revenue and Adjusted EBITDAR for Casinos & Resorts, North America Interactive, Bally's Intralot B2C and B2B, and the temporary Chicago casino (also reported separately by Bally's Chicago, Inc. in its own 10-Q); Sam's Town Shreveport's purchase price and contribution; cash held at Bally's Intralot against the consolidated total; one-off items (transaction, development, Star Entertainment fair value, debt extinguishment); and the reactions of the five covering brokers (Truist, Citizens, Stifel, Barclays and one restricted house), Deutsche Bank's credit desk, the rating agencies, the prices of the 5.625% 2029 and 5.875% 2031 senior notes, and Gaming and Leisure Properties. State what changed against the second-quarter 2026 10-Q (14 August 2026) and what remains undisclosed.

Request: OWN-2

Question: What exactly do Bally's Corporation's (BALY-US) regulatory and credit agreements restrict as its leverage covenants are reinstated in 2027, and what have comparable private-credit lenders and gaming regulators done in similar resets? Cover, first, the Amended and Restated Regulatory Agreement with the Rhode Island Department of Business Regulation and the Division of Lotteries: the precise definition of the 5.50 to 1.00 maximum leverage ratio (which debt and which EBITDA), whether Bally's has exceeded it since the February 2026 Twin River Lincoln sale-leaseback and the $1.1bn term loan, what payments it blocks when exceeded (dividends, buybacks, management fees, intercompany flows from Twin River Lincoln and Tiverton to the parent), the carve-outs, the regulator's discretion to raise the ratio, and any instance in which the Department has enforced, waived or modified it. Cover, second, the amended credit agreement (Bally's 8-K, 5 August 2026, and the February 2026 facility with Ares Management Credit, King Street Capital Management and TPG Credit): the first-lien net leverage covenant of 4.50 to 1.00 (4.00 to 1.00 after "Project Marathon") that returns with the compliance certificate for the quarter ending 31 March 2027; the rule that the test applies only when revolver utilisation exceeds 25% of commitments, and whether anti-window-dressing provisions limit quarter-end paydowns; the equity-cure rights (two in any four quarters) and who may fund them; the minimum-liquidity requirements after 31 December 2026, if any; the payment-in-kind election of up to 3.5% on the term loan and its effect on principal; the springing maturity of 1 March 2029 tied to the 5.625% senior notes due 2029; and the pledge of the Bally's Intralot shares. Cover, third, precedents: how Ares, King Street, TPG, WhiteHawk and similar private-credit lenders have handled covenant resets and amend-and-extend requests from gaming, leisure and other levered borrowers since 2023 (fees, margin step-ups, PIK, collateral, equity requirements, board rights, forbearance), and how gaming regulators in Rhode Island, Illinois and New York have treated licensees that breached financial covenants or disclosed going-concern doubt.

Request: OWN-3

Question: What cash, timing and licensing exposures does Bally's Corporation (BALY-US) face at its Chicago casino between December 2026 and the permanent casino's opening, and how are they likely to be resolved? Cover: the host community agreement's section 7.4 liquidated damages (85% of the projected daily city tax less actual tax if 75% of the gaming area is not open by the deadline), the definition and amount of the "Projected Per-Diem", the 10 December 2026 deadline, the two three-month extensions and one two-month extension the city may grant, whether any extension has been requested or granted, and the city's incentives given its 2026 budget; the temporary casino's four-year owner's licence expiring on 25 October 2027 and the twelve-month extension Bally's sought in January 2026; the $4.0m annual host community impact fee Bally's reportedly withheld in August 2026 and the legal remedies each side has threatened over the video gaming terminals (the Illinois Gaming Board had licensed 78 Chicago locations by late October 2026 and the city had issued no permits); the Class 7b property-tax incentive (reducing the assessment from 25% to 10%) that Bally's has sought and the mayor has opposed, its status and timetable; the Illinois Gambling Act reconciliation fee (75% of adjusted gross receipts in the most lucrative twelve months less initial position fees, due three years after operations commenced in September 2023, payable over six years) and the basis on which it will be assessed; the remaining spend under the $1.34bn minimum capital commitment, the expected overrun, the unexecuted subcontracts, and the pace of Gaming and Leisure Properties' remaining funding ($299.3m of $940m at 30 September 2026); the hotel tower and amenity sequencing after the August 2026 pause; and what Bally's Chicago, Inc.'s own SEC filings disclose that Bally's Corporation's do not. Compare with how Chicago treated the temporary casino's shortfall against the city's 2024 projections, and with how other Illinois and New York licensees (Hard Rock Rockford, Wind Creek Chicago Southland, Resorts World, the downstate New York licensees) handled opening deadlines, licence extensions and tax incentives.
